# Instructions

## General Instructions

**⚠️ CRITICAL — the Kubernetes rules in this file are triggered by the STEP TYPES in your output, not by the project type in the prompt**: A project the prompt calls a "wave deployment", "canary", "progressive", "LLM", "orchestration", or "Argo CD" project is still subject to every Kubernetes rule below the moment its output contains `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, or `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`. Never skip the Kubernetes target pattern or the Kubernetes step properties on the grounds that "this is not a Kubernetes project".

**⚠️ CRITICAL — Template default accounts (`account_octopussamples_azure_account`, `account_aws_oidc`) MUST NOT be created when the prompt names a specific account**: When the prompt says "Add an Azure OIDC account named 'X'" or "Create an Azure account called 'X'" or "Add an Azure Service Principal account called 'X'", the `octopusdeploy_azure_service_principal` resource named `account_octopussamples_azure_account` MUST NOT appear anywhere in the Terraform output. When the prompt says "Add an AWS account named 'X'", the `octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account` resource named `account_aws_oidc` MUST NOT appear anywhere in the output. These template defaults MUST be completely omitted and ALL references (including step `execution_properties`, project variables such as `Project.Azure.Account`, and any variable referencing an account) must use the prompt-specified account. Specifically: the `Project.Azure.Account` variable value MUST reference the prompt-specified account resource, not the template default account. This rule has ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over any template that includes these default accounts.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — template default account exclusion**: Before finalizing Terraform output, scan for any resource named `account_octopussamples_azure_account` or `account_aws_oidc`. If the prompt named a specific account, REMOVE these resources entirely and replace ALL references to them with references to the prompt-specified account resource. You will be penalized for each occurrence of `account_octopussamples_azure_account` or `account_aws_oidc` in the output when the prompt names a specific account.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — Prompt-specified deployment package feed MUST be used for packages, not the template default feed**: When the prompt says "Add a Docker container registry feed called 'X'" or "use the 'X' feed for deployment", you MUST create the prompt-specified feed resource AND use it for all `packages` references in steps. The template default `feed_github_container_registry` (GitHub Container Registry) is only permitted as the `container` block feed (worker tools container for step execution) — it MUST NOT be used as the deployment package `feed_id` when the prompt names a specific Docker feed. The prompt-specified feed MUST be created in addition to the `feed_github_container_registry` resource which may still be required for Kubernetes worker tool containers. Similarly, when a prompt names a specific Maven feed or Helm feed, the prompt-specified feed MUST be created and used for packages, even if the template default `octopusdeploy_maven_feed` resource named `feed_octopus_maven_feed` is also present.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — prompt-specified deployment feed**: Before finalizing Terraform output, scan the prompt for "Add a feed named 'X'" or "use the 'X' feed for..." instructions. For EACH such instruction, verify that: (1) a feed resource with `name = "X"` exists in the output, and (2) all `packages` blocks in steps that should use this feed have `feed_id` pointing to this prompt-specified feed, NOT to `feed_github_container_registry`. You will be penalized for each case where a deployment package uses `feed_github_container_registry` when the prompt specified a different feed.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — a step whose template hardcodes its image INLINE has NO `packages` block, so the check above passes vacuously and the prompt's feed ends up wired to nothing.** When the project type template writes the container image straight into the YAML — `image: octopussamples/randomquotes`, with `Packages: []` on the action — there is no `feed_id` anywhere for the self-check to inspect, so a prompt that named a feed produces a feed resource that no step, package or variable references. The feed exists, the check finds no offending `feed_id`, and the instruction was still not carried out.

* **Observed output — Progressive Deployment template.** The prompt said `Create a Docker container registry feed called "Commerce Registry" ... and use it for the container image "octopussamples/randomquotes" in the Kubernetes deployment step`. Output: `Commerce Registry` created, and the `Deploy App` step left with `Packages: []` and `image: octopussamples/randomquotes` hardcoded in `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml`. The feed governs nothing and, because nothing references it, it is not even exported with the project.
* **The fix is to ADD the package reference the template lacks.** When the prompt names a feed for an image, the step MUST gain a `packages` block for that image on the prompt's feed, and the YAML MUST reference the acquired package rather than a literal tag:

```hcl
packages = {
  randomquotes = {
    package_id           = "octopussamples/randomquotes"
    feed_id              = "${...feed_commerce_registry...}"
    acquisition_location = "NotAcquired"
    properties           = { Extract = "False", Purpose = "DockerImageReference", SelectionMode = "immediate" }
  }
}
```
```yaml
image: "octopussamples/randomquotes:#{Octopus.Action.Package[randomquotes].PackageVersion}"
```

* **⚠️ CRITICAL — the KEY of the `packages` map is a package REFERENCE NAME, not the package id, and Octopus validates its characters.** A reference name may contain only letters, numbers, periods, commas, dashes, underscores and hashes. A Docker image id (`octopussamples/randomquotes`) and a Maven coordinate (`com.octopus:ledger-batch`) both contain characters that are ILLEGAL in a reference name — `/` and `:` — so neither may be used as the key. Copy the id into `package_id` only, and derive the key from the last path segment with any remaining illegal characters removed: `octopussamples/randomquotes` → `randomquotes`, `com.octopus:ledger-batch` → `ledger-batch`.
* The failure is at CREATE time, not plan time, so it is maximally destructive: the project, its variables and its tenants are created and the step that carries the package — and every step after it — is not.

```
Error: Unable to create process step

  with octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_checkout_canary_deploy_canary[0],

Octopus API error: There was a problem with your request. [Package name
'octopussamples/randomquotes' contains invalid characters. Names can only
contain letters, numbers, periods, commas, dashes, underscores or hashes.]
```

* **Observed output.** A canary prompt saying `use it for the container image "octopussamples/randomquotes" in the Kubernetes deployment steps` produced `packages = { "octopussamples/randomquotes" = { package_id = "octopussamples/randomquotes" ... } }`. The project was left with no deployment process at all. Writing the id in both positions is the natural mistake when the prompt names the image once — the two fields are NOT the same field.
* Whatever key you choose MUST also be the name used in the YAML: `#{Octopus.Action.Package[<key>].PackageVersion}`. A key of `randomquotes` and a YAML reference to `Package[octopussamples/randomquotes]` is a dangling reference even though the apply succeeds.

* This is exactly what the Kubernetes project type template already does; the Progressive Deployment, canary and wave templates do not, and that difference is the whole trap. Do not conclude that "the template has no package block" means "the prompt's feed has nowhere to go".
* The same reasoning applies to any prompt-named supporting resource: creating it is only half the instruction. An account, certificate, feed or worker pool that no step, variable or target references has not been "used", however plainly it appears in the output.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — prompt-named feed is actually referenced**: before finalizing output, for EACH feed the prompt named, search the whole output for its resource address. If the only occurrence is the feed's own `resource` block and its `data` lookup, the feed is orphaned — find the step the prompt tied it to and add the `packages` block (and the `#{Octopus.Action.Package[...]}` YAML reference) that consumes it.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — Prompt-specified accounts MUST be created**: When the prompt says "Add an AWS OIDC account called 'X'" or "Add an Azure account called 'X'", you MUST create the corresponding account resource (`octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account` or `octopusdeploy_azure_openid_connect`) with the exact name specified in the prompt. Failing to create a prompt-specified account is always wrong. You will be penalized for each prompt-specified account that is absent from the Terraform output. This rule applies regardless of the project template being used — even if the Kubernetes, Azure, or other template does not include an account, the prompt-specified account MUST STILL be created as a separate top-level resource. Example: if the prompt says "Add an AWS OIDC account called 'AWS Deploy Account' with the role ARN 'arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/OctopusDeployRole'", the following resource MUST appear in the output:
```
resource "octopusdeploy_aws_openid_connect_account" "account_aws_deploy_account" {
  name                              = "AWS Deploy Account"
  role_arn                          = "arn:aws:iam::123456789012:role/OctopusDeployRole"
  account_test_subject_keys         = ["space"]
  execution_subject_keys            = ["space"]
  health_subject_keys               = ["space"]
  session_duration                  = 3600
  environments                      = []
  tenant_tags                       = []
  tenants                           = []
  tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"
}
```

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — prompt-specified account creation**: Before finalizing Terraform output, scan the prompt for any "Add an ... account called 'X'" or "Create an ... account named 'X'" instructions. For EACH such instruction, verify that a corresponding account resource with `name = "X"` exists in the Terraform output. If any prompt-specified account is missing, add it immediately. This check MUST be performed even for Kubernetes projects where the template does not include accounts.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — every `#{VariableName}` referenced anywhere in the output MUST have a matching variable resource**: A step property, script body, or manifest that references `#{Foo}` when no `Foo` variable exists produces a dangling reference. Octopus accepts it silently — the apply succeeds and the substitution resolves to the literal text `#{Foo}` at deployment time, which is very hard to diagnose. Whenever you invent a variable reference to satisfy a required property, you MUST also create the variable in the same output. Octopus system variables in the `Octopus.` namespace are provided by the server and MUST NOT be created. Variables supplied by a linked library variable set, or by a tenant variable template declared on the project, also satisfy the reference.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — no dangling variable references**: Before finalizing Terraform output, list every `#{...}` token that appears in any `execution_properties` value, script body, or inline YAML. For each token that is not in the `Octopus.` namespace, confirm that an `octopusdeploy_variable` resource, a linked library variable set, or a tenant variable template supplies it. Create any that are missing. This includes `#{...}` tokens you copied out of a community step template's parameter defaults — for example `BlueGreen.Octopus.Api.Key = "#{Project.Octopus.Api.Key}"` requires a `Project.Octopus.Api.Key` variable to exist.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — the check above applies to `#{...}` tokens INHERITED FROM THE PROJECT TYPE TEMPLATE, not only to ones you invented.** A template's script body can reference a variable the template itself never defines, and copying the step without also creating the variable carries the dangling reference into every project you generate. The template is not self-consistent here and MUST be corrected as you emit it.

* **Known case — the AWS Lambda template.** Its `Validate Setup` step contains `if ([string]::IsNullOrWhitespace("#{Project.AWS.Lambda.FunctionName}")) { $errors += "The project variable Project.AWS.Lambda.FunctionName has not been configured." }`, but no `Project.AWS.Lambda.FunctionName` variable exists in the template's variable list (which supplies only `Project.AWS.Lambda.S3.BucketName`, `Project.AWS.Lambda.S3.FileName`, `Project.AWS.Region`, `Project.AWS.Account`, `Resources:ProductsMicroservice:Properties:CodeUri`, `Project.WorkerPool` and `Project.Octopus.Api.Key`). You MUST therefore also emit a `Project.AWS.Lambda.FunctionName` variable — a `String` whose value is the Lambda function name, derived from the project name when the prompt does not give one.
* The failure is silent and self-defeating: an undefined variable is left as the literal text `#{Project.AWS.Lambda.FunctionName}`, which is NOT null or whitespace, so the validation guard reports success and the deployment proceeds with no function name configured. A validation step that cannot fail is worse than no validation step.
* Treat this as the general pattern: whenever a template's script *checks whether a variable is configured*, that variable MUST appear in the output's variable list.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — ANY project containing a Kubernetes step MUST create a "Mock K8s" deployment target, and every raw-YAML step MUST override three step properties. This rule has ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over any template, and over the fact that the prompt never mentioned a target.** A prompt that only names a target tag ("on targets tagged `insight-cluster`"), or names a cluster, or asks for a Kubernetes/canary/progressive/wave/blue-green deployment, is a prompt that needs this pattern. A tag says *which* target to use; it does not create one. A project with `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, or `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` steps and no Kubernetes target cannot deploy at all. You MUST therefore emit ALL FOUR of the following:

1. An `octopusdeploy_token_account` called "Mock Token" (plus its `data "octopusdeploy_accounts"` lookup and `count`). Do not substitute an AWS, Azure, or username/password account. If the prompt names its own Token account, use that one instead and do not create "Mock Token". **Most prompts never mention an account at all, and that is the single most common reason this whole pattern gets skipped — do not let it stop you.** The target needs a Token account to authenticate, so when the prompt is silent about accounts you MUST invent "Mock Token" yourself and carry on to items 2 and 3. "The prompt did not ask for an account" is NEVER a valid reason to omit the account, and omitting the account is NEVER a valid reason to omit the target.
2. An `octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry` feed called "Docker Hub" pointing at `https://index.docker.io` with NO credentials.
3. An `octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target` called "Mock K8s" with `cluster_url = "https://mockk8s.octopusdemos.com"`, `roles` containing EVERY target tag used by the project's Kubernetes steps plus `"Kubernetes"`, `environments` listing every environment in the project's lifecycle, an `endpoint { communication_style = "Kubernetes" }` block, an `authentication { account_id = … }` block referencing the token account, and the "Hosted Ubuntu" worker pool as `default_worker_pool_id`.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — Kubernetes target:** before finalizing output, search your Terraform for `Octopus.Kubernetes` and `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`. If there is even one match, confirm all three items above are present, then confirm `octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target` appears exactly once. If it does not appear, STOP and add it before responding. The full pattern, including the canonical HCL, is under `## Kubernetes target` in the `# Patterns` section.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — EVERY raw-YAML Kubernetes step MUST set all three "Mock K8s" compatibility properties. This is a SEPARATE requirement from creating the target, and it is the one most often forgotten.** Whenever the project uses the "Mock K8s" target, add ALL THREE of these entries to the `execution_properties` of EVERY step whose type is `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`:

```
"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck"      = "False"
"Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait" = "NoWait"
"Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled"  = "False"
```

* "Mock K8s" supports none of these three features. `ResourceStatusCheck = "True"`, `ServerSideApply.Enabled = "True"`, or a missing `DeploymentWait` each guarantee the deployment fails at runtime.
* **⚠️ These three properties belong to `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` steps ONLY. They are NOT valid on `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` (kubectl script) steps, and NOT valid on `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` steps.** A kubectl script step runs the script body you give it and applies nothing itself, so there is no manifest to status-check, no rollout to wait on, and no apply mode to choose; the same is true of a Helm step, which drives Helm rather than `kubectl apply`. Writing any of the three keys into those step types adds properties the step does not read. Do NOT "fix" a kubectl script or Helm step by adding them, and do NOT count those steps when checking that every step carries the triple.
* The `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` and `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` step types still require the "Mock K8s" TARGET — that requirement covers every Kubernetes step type without exception. It is only these three *step properties* that are raw-YAML-only. Do not collapse the two rules into one.
* Copying these values from the example configuration is not enough — the templates ship with `ResourceStatusCheck = "True"` and `ServerSideApply.Enabled = "True"`, so you MUST overwrite them.
* **⚠️ A raw-YAML step the PROMPT adds needs these three properties just as much as a step inherited from the template, and this is where they are most often missing.** The template's raw-YAML steps arrive with the properties already written, so correcting them feels like the whole job; a step such as "Add a 'Deploy raw Kubernetes YAML' step called 'Deploy Canary'" starts from nothing and gets an `execution_properties` block containing only `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.CustomResourceYaml`, `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource` and `Octopus.Action.RunOnServer`. Write all three compatibility keys into that block too. A prompt instruction phrased over "the Kubernetes steps", "the K8s steps" or "the Kubernetes step" is PLURAL in effect: it governs every raw-YAML step in the finished process, including the ones the prompt itself introduces later in the same list of instructions — never read it as applying only to the template's steps. It does NOT, however, reach kubectl script or Helm steps: those step types never carry the triple, no matter how the instruction is phrased.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — Kubernetes step properties:** list every step in your output whose type is exactly `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`. For each one, read its `execution_properties` and confirm all three keys above are present with exactly the values `"False"`, `"NoWait"`, and `"False"`. Count the raw-YAML steps and count the property triples — the numbers MUST match. Add whatever is missing before responding. Walk the list in the order the steps appear in `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`, not in template-first order, so the prompt-added steps at the end of the process are checked rather than skipped. Then make the reverse pass: list every `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` and `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` step and confirm that NONE of them carries `ResourceStatusCheck`, `DeploymentWait`, or `ServerSideApply.Enabled` — delete any you find.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — the "Mock K8s" target's `container` block MUST use a Docker registry feed, never the built-in feed**: the health check container `octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.5.0-ubuntu.22.04` cannot be pulled from `Octopus Server (built-in)`. Create an `octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry` feed called "Docker Hub" pointing at `https://index.docker.io` with NO credentials, and set the target's `container { feed_id = … image = "octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.5.0-ubuntu.22.04" }` to that feed. Never leave `feed_id` empty, never point it at `data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_octopus_server_built_in`, and do not substitute a package feed the prompt asked for.

* **⚠️ "Do not substitute the prompt's feed" does NOT mean "reuse whichever registry feed the template already has".** The template supplies `feed_ghcr_anonymous` and `feed_github_container_registry`, both of which point at GitHub Container Registry, and reaching for one of them is the usual way this rule is broken — it looks like reuse rather than substitution, and it avoids creating a resource the prompt never mentioned. It is still wrong: `octopusdeploy/worker-tools` is a Docker Hub image and GHCR cannot serve it, so the target's health check fails to pull. The "Docker Hub" feed is required by the TARGET, not by the prompt, and MUST be created even when the prompt names no feed at all and even when the prompt names a different Docker feed.
* **Observed output.** A Kubernetes prompt that asked for a Docker feed called `Insight Registry` at `https://index.docker.io` produced `Insight Registry` (correctly used for the application image) and a "Mock K8s" target whose `container` block pointed at the template's `GHCR Anonymous` feed. No feed named "Docker Hub" existed anywhere in the output. Note the irony to avoid repeating: the prompt's own feed already pointed at `https://index.docker.io`, and the template feed chosen instead did not.
* The distinction is by ROLE, not by registry URL: the deployment package feed comes from the prompt; a step's `container` block feed is the GitHub Container Registry feed; the target's `container` block feed is the anonymous "Docker Hub" feed. Three separate feeds, and one of them being absent is never fixed by borrowing another.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — when the project is tenanted, the "Mock K8s" target MUST be linked to the project's tenants**: a target with `tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"` is invisible to a tenanted deployment, so the project has no target at all for its tenants. When the project's `tenanted_deployment_participation` is `Tenanted` or `TenantedOrUntenanted`, the target MUST use the same value, and its `tenants` list (or `tenant_tags` list) MUST include every tenant the prompt linked to the project. Any Token account created for the target must be linked to those tenants too.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — EVERY `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource MUST have a `count` attribute and a matching `data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles"` lookup**, exactly like every other resource in a new project. This applies to every lifecycle, including a second "hotfix" lifecycle used only by a channel.

* **⚠️ BEWARE THE NAME COLLISION: `lifecycle { }` inside a resource is TERRAFORM'S META-BLOCK, not the Octopus lifecycle.** The Terraform `lifecycle { }` meta-block accepts ONLY `prevent_destroy`, `ignore_changes` and `create_before_destroy`. `release_retention_with_strategy` and `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` MUST NEVER be written inside it. Because the Octopus resource is *also* called `octopusdeploy_lifecycle`, it is easy to put the retention blocks inside the `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }` meta-block by mistake — which fails the plan with `Blocks of type "release_retention_with_strategy" are not expected here`, reported against the enclosing resource so the real location is not obvious. The `lifecycle { }` meta-block is separate and contains only its own three arguments:

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_my_lifecycle" {
  count = "..."
  name  = "My Lifecycle"

  # CORRECT — NOT inside the Terraform lifecycle {} meta-block. Always write these MULTI-LINE, never on one line.
  release_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }

  tentacle_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }

  phase { ... }

  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true      # ONLY prevent_destroy / ignore_changes / create_before_destroy belong here
  }
}
```
* The `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource MUST have `count` and a matching `data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles"` lookup, like every other resource in a new project. A lifecycle emitted without `count` is a reliable sign you have hand-written it instead of following the canonical shape, and it is in that hand-written form that this error appears. The canonical shape is under `## Lifecycle Phase` / `## Retention Policy for New Project Creation Prompts` — follow it literally.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — a retention policy MUST NEVER be the reason a project fails to be created. If you cannot express it, OMIT IT.** Creating the project is the objective; the retention policy is a refinement of it. If you are at all unsure how to write `release_retention_with_strategy` or `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` for a lifecycle — the exact block or attribute syntax, where it nests, or which unit applies — then leave BOTH of them out of the `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource entirely and generate everything else. Octopus applies a sensible default retention when the attributes are absent, so a lifecycle with no retention policy is a small, cosmetic shortfall in an otherwise complete project.

By contrast, a malformed retention policy fails at PLAN time, which means no project, no steps, no variables, no tenants — nothing at all is created and the whole prompt is wasted. That outcome is far worse than a missing retention setting. The same applies to `retention_policy` on `octopusdeploy_runbook`: if in doubt, omit the block rather than risk the apply.

**Order of preference, highest to lowest:** (1) a complete project with a correct retention policy; (2) a complete project with NO retention policy; (3) nothing at all because the plan failed. Never choose (3) by attempting (1) when you are unsure. If a previous attempt at this prompt failed with `Unsupported block type` naming `release_retention_with_strategy` or `tentacle_retention_with_strategy`, omit both blocks on the next attempt.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — OMIT the `retention_policy` block from `octopusdeploy_runbook` entirely**: the block is deprecated (`Runbook Retention Policies will soon require strategy`) and every way of filling it in breaks the run, so the only reliable choice is to leave it out and let Octopus apply its default.

The two ways it fails, both fatal:

```
Error: Invalid Attribute Combination

  on terraformInput.tf line 708, in resource "octopusdeploy_runbook" "runbook_registry_search_rebuild_search_index":
 708:     should_keep_forever = true

Attribute "retention_policy[0].quantity_to_keep" cannot be specified when
"retention_policy[0].should_keep_forever" is specified
```

```
Error: Provider produced inconsistent result after apply

When applying changes to octopusdeploy_runbook.runbook_14__claims_intake_site_recycle_claims_app_pool[0],
provider "registry.opentofu.org/octopusdeploy/octopusdeploy" produced an unexpected new value:
.retention_policy[0].quantity_to_keep: was cty.NumberIntVal(100), but now cty.NumberIntVal(0).
```

* `quantity_to_keep` and `should_keep_forever` are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE — specifying both fails at plan time. Never write them together.
* Specifying `quantity_to_keep = N` with `should_keep_forever = false` gets normalised by Octopus to `0` and aborts the apply.
* Therefore: do not emit a `retention_policy` block on a runbook at all. If you judge one to be unavoidable, use `should_keep_forever = true` ALONE, with no `quantity_to_keep` line.
* A retention instruction in the prompt applies to the LIFECYCLE (`octopusdeploy_lifecycle`), never to runbooks — do not copy the lifecycle's `quantity_to_keep` onto a runbook.

**When the prompt explicitly asks to keep a number of RUNBOOK RUNS, express it with `run_retention_policy_with_strategy` — never with the deprecated `retention_policy` block.** A prompt such as `Add a runbook called "Recycle Claims App Pool" ... and keep the last 100 runbook runs` is asking about runbook runs specifically, not about releases, so redirecting it to the lifecycle would be wrong. The strategy block is the current, non-deprecated form and has been observed to apply cleanly:

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_runbook" "runbook_recycle_claims_app_pool" {
  count = "..."
  name  = "Recycle Claims App Pool"

  run_retention_policy_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 100
    unit             = "Items"
  }
}
```

* `unit` is `"Items"` when the prompt counts RUNS, and `"Days"` only when the prompt counts days.
* Never write both `run_retention_policy_with_strategy` and `retention_policy` on the same runbook.
* This does NOT relax the rule above: an unrequested retention setting still belongs nowhere, and the deprecated `retention_policy` block remains forbidden in every case. If you are unsure of the strategy block's syntax, omit retention from the runbook entirely rather than risk the apply.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — `minimum_environments_before_promotion` MUST NEVER exceed the number of environments in its OWN `phase` block, and a "deployments" count in the prompt NEVER sets it.** This is repeated here, at the top of the file, because it is the single cheapest way to lose an entire project: the lifecycle is created early, so when Octopus rejects it the apply aborts before the project, its steps, its variables and its feeds are ever created, and the prompt produces NOTHING.

```
Error: unable to create lifecycle

  with octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_claims_portal_lifecycle[0],

Octopus API error: There was a problem with your request. [The phase "Claims Staging" requires a minimum of 2 environments for promotion, but only 1 environments have been added. Please add more environments or reduce the minimum required.]
```

* The attribute counts environments *inside its own phase*. A phase holding ONE environment admits ONLY `0` or `1` — never 2, 3, or any number lifted from the prompt.
* **The trap is a phrase that counts DEPLOYMENTS and names a DIFFERENT phase**, such as `a "Claims Staging" phase that automatically promotes releases after 2 successful deployments to "Claims Dev"`. Observed output: `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 2` on the single-environment `Claims Staging` phase, killing the apply. Three separate mistakes are folded into that one line — the number counts deployments rather than environments, it belongs (if anywhere) to the `Claims Dev` phase rather than the `Claims Staging` phase, and it exceeds the phase's environment count. Octopus has no "promote after N successful deployments" feature at all: set `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0` and ignore the number.
* Before emitting ANY `phase` block, count the entries in that block's `automatic_deployment_targets` plus `optional_deployment_targets`. If `minimum_environments_before_promotion` is greater than that count, it is wrong — write `0`.
* The detailed rule, with worked examples, is under `## Lifecycle Phase `minimum_environments_before_promotion` Instructions`. That section exists and has still been violated in practice, which is why the rule is restated here.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "false"` aborts the apply on any step Octopus decides runs on the server or a worker**: Octopus recomputes this property when it stores the action. When it comes back as `"true"` and you declared `"false"`, the provider treats it as drift and kills the apply:

```
Error: Provider produced inconsistent result after apply

When applying changes to octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_14__claims_intake_site_stop_claims_app_pool[0],
provider "registry.opentofu.org/octopusdeploy/octopusdeploy" produced an unexpected new value:
.execution_properties["Octopus.Action.RunOnServer"]: was cty.StringVal("false"), but now cty.StringVal("true").
```

This is one of the most damaging failures in this file, because process steps are created early: the first step that trips it aborts everything after it, and the project is left with one step and none of its remaining steps, feeds, or tenant resources.

* For an `Octopus.Script` step, set `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`. This is what the example configurations use in almost every case, and it is always safe.
* NEVER set `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "false"` on a runbook script step, a manual intervention step, a webhook/curl step, or any step that does not carry `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles`. Those all run on the server, so `"false"` is guaranteed to be rewritten to `"true"` and guaranteed to abort the apply.
* **`"false"` is FORBIDDEN on EVERY step of EVERY runbook, without exception and regardless of step type or wording.** Any `octopusdeploy_process_step` whose `process_id` points at a runbook's `octopusdeploy_process` MUST use `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"`. This holds even when the runbook plainly acts on a machine — "restart the service", "drain connections from the target", "recycle the app pool" all describe work aimed at a target, but the runbook step itself is still orchestrated server-side. A runbook whose only step trips this error is created EMPTY, which is a broken runbook.
* **"runs on a worker" means `"true"`, NOT `"false"`.** When a prompt says a step should run "on a worker", "on a worker rather than the target", "off the target", or "on the Octopus Server", set `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"` and select the worker via `worker_pool_id`/`worker_pool_variable`. Worker execution IS server-orchestrated execution. Reading "worker" as "not the server" and writing `"false"` is the single most common way this error is introduced.
* **A step may only use `"false"` if that same step also declares `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles`.** If you are about to write `"false"` on a step with no target roles, the value is wrong — change it to `"true"`. Do not copy a `"false"` from one step onto another step; each step's value depends on that step's own roles.
* Use `"false"` ONLY on a package-deployment step type — `Octopus.TentaclePackage`, `Octopus.WindowsService`, `Octopus.IIS`, `Octopus.TomcatDeploy` — that also has target roles. When in doubt, prefer `"true"`.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — OMITTING `Octopus.Action.RunOnServer` from a script step aborts the apply just as surely as setting it to `"false"`. Never leave it out.** Octopus adds the property itself when it stores a server-orchestrated action, so a step whose `execution_properties` never mentioned it comes back carrying it, and the provider rejects the extra key:

```
Error: Provider produced inconsistent result after apply

When applying changes to octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_claims_portal_restart_tomcat_restart_tomcat[0],
provider "registry.opentofu.org/octopusdeploy/octopusdeploy" produced an unexpected new value:
.execution_properties: new element "Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" has appeared.
```

* This variant is triggered most often by a runbook script step that the prompt described in one line ("add a runbook called 'Restart Tomcat' with a script step that echoes 'Restarting Tomcat'"). Such a step is easy to write as a minimal `execution_properties` block holding only `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody`, `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptSource` and `Octopus.Action.Script.Syntax` — and that minimal block is exactly what fails.
* Therefore EVERY `Octopus.Script` step and EVERY step of EVERY runbook MUST state `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"` explicitly. "Let Octopus choose" is not an available option: the property is written either by you (apply succeeds) or by Octopus after the fact (apply fails).
* The consequence is the same as the `"false"` variant and just as expensive — the runbook is created with an empty process and every resource ordered after the failing step is abandoned.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — RunOnServer**: perform BOTH checks.
1. Search your output for `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "false"`. For every hit, confirm ALL THREE of: (1) the step is a package-deployment step type, (2) the SAME step declares `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles`, and (3) the step belongs to the project's deployment process, not to a runbook process. If any one of those fails, change the value to `"true"`. If it is a script, manual, or runbook step, change the value to `"true"`.
2. List every `octopusdeploy_process_step` in your output and read its `execution_properties`. Any step of type `Octopus.Script`, `Octopus.Manual`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, or any step belonging to a runbook process, whose `execution_properties` has NO `Octopus.Action.RunOnServer` key at all, is missing it — add `"Octopus.Action.RunOnServer" = "true"` before responding.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — an `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` MUST NOT declare a `parameters` entry whose value is an empty string**: Octopus discards empty parameter values when it stores the action, so the provider compares what it sent against what came back, finds the key gone, and aborts the whole apply:

```
Error: Provider produced inconsistent result after apply

When applying changes to octopusdeploy_process_templated_step.process_step_loyalty_rewards_promote_next_wave[0],
provider "registry.opentofu.org/octopusdeploy/octopusdeploy" produced an
unexpected new value: .parameters: element "Run.Runbook.PromptedVariables" has vanished.
```

A community step template usually declares a dozen or more optional parameters. Set ONLY the ones you have a real value for, and OMIT every other key entirely — do not add `"Run.Runbook.PromptedVariables" = ""`, `"Run.Runbook.Tenant.Name" = ""`, `"Run.Runbook.CustomNotes" = ""`, or any other `"…" = ""` entry "for completeness". An omitted optional parameter is correct; an empty-string parameter destroys the project. This applies to every community step template, not just "Run Octopus Deploy Runbook".

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — no empty templated-step parameters**: before finalizing output, read every `parameters` block on every `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` and delete any entry whose value is `""`.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — an unscoped `octopusdeploy_variable` MUST OMIT the `scope` block completely**: A `scope {}` block, or a `scope` block whose every attribute is `null`, aborts the whole apply with `Error: Provider produced inconsistent result after apply … .scope: block count changed from 1 to 0`, so the project is left half-built. Never emit a placeholder `scope` block "for consistency" — a variable with no scope has no `scope` block. This rule has ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over any template.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — `actions` in a variable `scope` block MUST ALWAYS be `null`, no matter what the prompt asks for**: When the prompt says a variable is `scoped to the "<Step Name>" step`, you MUST NOT try to express that. `octopusdeploy_process_step.<name>.id` is the STEP id; Octopus's `Scope.Actions` needs the ACTION id, which Terraform never exposes. Writing `actions = [octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_deploy_fraud_lambda.id]` produces a variable scoped to a GUID that matches nothing: the apply succeeds silently, and the variable is then either discarded outright or kept with a dangling scope that can never match a deployment. Both outcomes mean the value never applies.

* Emit the variable with NO `actions` scope. Use the environments, channels, target tags, or tenant tags the prompt also gave; if it gave none, emit the variable with no `scope` block at all.
* Do NOT drop the variable, and do NOT substitute a `roles` scope you invented.
* This rule has ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over the prompt's scoping instruction. Losing the step scope is expected and correct; losing the variable is not.

* **⚠️ `processes` IS NOT AN ESCAPE HATCH FOR THE STEP SCOPE YOU WERE JUST TOLD TO DROP.** Having accepted that `actions` cannot carry a step id, the next move is to write that same step id into `processes` instead — the attribute is permitted, the plan is clean, the apply succeeds, and the result is the identical dangling scope. `processes` scopes a variable to a PROCESS OWNER, so its only legal values are a PROJECT id (`octopusdeploy_project.<name>[0].id`) or a RUNBOOK id (`octopusdeploy_runbook.<name>[0].id`). An `octopusdeploy_process_step` id is neither.
* **Observed output.** A prompt saying `Create a variable called "Claims.AppPool.Name" with the value "ClaimsAppPool" scoped to the "Stop Claims App Pool" step` produced `scope { processes = ["${octopusdeploy_process_step.process_step_claims_intake_site_stop_claims_app_pool.id}"] }`. The apply reported success and the stored variable came back as `"Scope": { "ProcessOwner": ["e311ba10-1e9b-4471-b83e-8fc7cd49751a"] }` — the step's own GUID sitting in a field Octopus resolves against project and runbook ids, matching nothing. The variable can never apply to any deployment, and nothing in the apply output says so.
* The general rule: NO scope attribute may hold an `octopusdeploy_process_step` or `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` id. Not `actions`, not `processes`, not `machines`, not `roles`. A step-scoped variable is simply not expressible — emit the variable unscoped (or scoped only by the environments/channels/tenant tags the prompt also gave) and move on.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — no step ids in any scope**: search your output for every `scope` block. Confirm `actions` is `null` or absent in every one. Then read every `processes` value and confirm each entry references an `octopusdeploy_project` or `octopusdeploy_runbook` resource id — if any entry references an `octopusdeploy_process_step` or `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step`, delete the `processes` line (and the whole `scope` block if that leaves it empty). Do the same for `machines` and `roles`. A `scope` block left with no non-null attribute MUST be removed entirely.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — a tenant variable TEMPLATE is not a tenant variable VALUE; do not emit `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` without a value from the prompt**: An `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` whose `value` is empty aborts the apply at create time with `Error: Failed to get variable ID … Variable was created but ID not returned in response`, and every resource ordered after it is abandoned. When the prompt says only "add a project tenant variable template called 'X'" it is asking for a `template` block on the `octopusdeploy_project` resource and NOTHING ELSE. Create `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` only when the prompt states an actual value for a named tenant. This rule has ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over any template.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — a Blue/Green project MUST have TWO DISTINCT production environments, even when the prompt names only one**: The `Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment` step compares the last two production deployments across two separate environments, so `BlueGreen.Environment.Blue.Name` and `BlueGreen.Environment.Green.Name` MUST hold two DIFFERENT environment names. Setting both to `Production` (or to any single environment the prompt happened to name) makes the check meaningless and is always wrong. A prompt that asks for a blue/green strategy but lists only a `Production` environment is still a blue/green prompt: replace that single environment with `Blue Production` and `Green Production`, put EACH of them in its own `phase` (exactly one environment per phase, both `is_optional_phase = true`), and reference them from the two step parameters. Do NOT merge the two stacks into a single `Production` phase — separate optional phases are what let a release be promoted to one stack without the other. This rule has ABSOLUTE PRIORITY over the environment list in the prompt and over any template.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — `depends_on` for sequential steps MUST reference ONLY the immediately preceding step**: Each `octopusdeploy_process_step` resource (steps 2, 3, 4, etc.) must have `depends_on` pointing to EXACTLY ONE step — the step that immediately precedes it in the deployment process. NEVER list all previous steps in `depends_on`. For step N, `depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_step.step_N_minus_1]`. For step N to depend on ALL previous steps violates the rule and creates unnecessary complexity. WRONG: `depends_on = [step_1, step_2, step_3, step_4, step_5, step_6]` for step 7. CORRECT: `depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_step.step_6]` for step 7.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — ALL resources in a new project creation MUST use `count` with data source lookup**: Every resource that represents a project-scoped or space-scoped entity created as part of a project MUST include `count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_<TYPE>s.<data_name>.<attribute>) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"` AND an associated `data "octopusdeploy_<TYPE>"` data source. This applies to the following resources in new project creation prompts:

- `octopusdeploy_project` — MUST have `count` and ternary `project_id`
- `octopusdeploy_process` — MUST have `count` and ternary `project_id`
- `octopusdeploy_process_step` / `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` — MUST have `count` and ternary `process_id`
- `octopusdeploy_variable` — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_environment` — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_channel` — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_project_scheduled_trigger` — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_community_step_template` — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_project_versioning_strategy` — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_tenant` — MUST have `count` (when tenant is requested)
- `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` — MUST have `count` (when tenant_project is requested)
- `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` / `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable` — MUST have `count` (when tenant variables are requested)
- `octopusdeploy_tag_set` — MUST have `count` (when tenant has tags)
- `octopusdeploy_tag` — MUST have `count` (when tenant has tags)
- `octopusdeploy_project_group` — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry` (feed) — MUST have `count`
- `octopusdeploy_maven_feed`, `octopusdeploy_nuget_feed`, etc. (feeds) — MUST have `count`

This pattern ensures idempotent re-apply: when the resource already exists, `count = 0` skips creation and the data source is used; when it doesn't exist, `count = 1` creates it.

**Project resource — CRITICAL `count` + data source pattern (MUST for new project creation)**:
The `octopusdeploy_project` resource MUST include a `count` attribute with a matching data source.

```hcl
# Data source to lookup existing project
data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project_my_project" {
  ids           = null
  partial_name = "My Project"
  skip          = 0
  take          = 1
}

variable "project_my_project_name" {
  type         = string
  nullable     = false
  sensitive    = false
  description = "The name of the project"
  default      = "My Project"
}

# Resource with count for idempotent re-apply
resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_my_project" {
  count                            = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                             = "${var.project_my_project_name}"
  lifecycle_id                     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_devsecops.lifecycles) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_devsecops.lifecycles[0].id : octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_devsecops[0].id}"
  project_group_id                 = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_project_groups.project_group_kubernetes.project_groups) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_project_groups.project_group_kubernetes.project_groups[0].id : octopusdeploy_project_group.project_group_kubernetes[0].id}"
  template_id                      = ""
  tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"
  is_version_controlled           = false
  web_page                        = ""
  default_worker_pool_id          = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_default_worker_pool.worker_pools) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.workerpool_default_worker_pool.worker_pools[0].id : octopusdeploy_worker_pool.workerpool_default_worker_pool[0].id}"
  default_tool_version            = "ToolVersionLatest"
}
```

**Process resource — CRITICAL `count` + ternary `project_id` (MUST for new project creation)**:
The `octopusdeploy_process` resource MUST include `count` and a ternary `project_id`.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process" "process_my_project" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  depends_on = []
}
```

**Process step / templated step — CRITICAL `count` + ternary `process_id` (MUST for new project creation)**:
Every `octopusdeploy_process_step` and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` resource MUST include `count` and a ternary `process_id`.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_first_step" {
  count        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name         = "First Step"
  type         = "Octopus.Script"
  process_id   = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_my_project[0].id}"
  # ... other attributes ...
}

resource "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step" "process_step_my_project_community_step" {
  count          = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name           = "Community Step"
  process_id     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_my_project[0].id}"
  template_id    = "${data.octopusdeploy_step_template.steptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.step_template != null ? data.octopusdeploy_step_template.steptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.step_template.id : octopusdeploy_community_step_template.communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities[0].id}"
  # ... other attributes ...
}
```

**Variable resource — CRITICAL `count` (MUST for new project creation)**:
Every `octopusdeploy_variable` resource MUST include `count`.

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "my_project_connection_string" {
  count        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  owner_id     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  name         = "Connection.String"
  type         = "String"
  value        = "server=prod"
  is_sensitive = false
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes = [sensitive_value]
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

**Environment resource — CRITICAL `count` + ternary `id` references (MUST for new project creation)**:
Every `octopusdeploy_environment` resource MUST include `count`, a matching data source, and `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }`.

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_environments" "environment_development" {

  skip          = 0
  take          = 1

  name  = "Development"
  # ... other attributes ...

    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```

**Lifecycle resource — CRITICAL `count` (MUST for new project creation)**:

```hcl
  }
```

**Channel resource — CRITICAL `count` with project-based lookup**:
Every `octopusdeploy_channel` resource MUST include `count` referencing a `data.octopusdeploy_projects` data source (NOT a channels data source).

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_channel" "channel_my_project_hotfix" {

  lifecycle_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_hotfix.lifecycles) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_hotfix.lifecycles[0].id : octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_hotfix[0].id}"

  project_id   = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  is_default   = false
  tenant_tags  = []
}
```

**Scheduled trigger — CRITICAL `count` (MUST for new project creation)**:
Every `octopusdeploy_project_scheduled_trigger` resource MUST include `count`.

**Worker pool lookup**:
When a step references a worker pool, use a data source + ternary:

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_worker_pools" "workerpool_default_worker_pool" {
  ids           = null
  partial_name = "Default Worker Pool"
  skip          = 0
  take          = 1
}
```

The step's `worker_pool_variable` should be set to `"Octopus.Project.WorkerPool"` (this is the correct value). The `default_worker_pool_id` on the project should use the ternary pattern shown above.

**Community step template — CRITICAL `count` with both data sources**:
Every `octopusdeploy_community_step_template` resource MUST include `count` and use BOTH the step_template and community_step_template data sources:

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_community_step_template" "communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities" {
  website = "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/a38bfff8-8dde-4dd6-9fd0-c90bb4709d5a"

  community_action_template_id       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_community_step_template.communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.steps) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_community_step_template.communitysteptemplate_scan_for_vulnerabilities.steps[0].id : null}"
```

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — count + data source completeness scan**: Before finalizing Terraform output, confirm that EVERY resource type listed above carries its `count` attribute, that each `count` and ternary expression references a data source that also appears in the output, that every `octopusdeploy_environment` and `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` also has `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }`, and that a `data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools` data source exists for any worker pool lookup. Fix anything missing BEFORE finalizing the Terraform output. You will be penalized for outputting Terraform that lacks the `count` + data source pattern on any resource listed above.

* You must carefully consider the prompt and create all the required resources.
* You must ignore any mention of the current project.
* If the target space is defined in the prompt, that must take precedence over any mention of a current space. For example:
  * When given the prompt `Create a project called 'Example Project' in the 'Example Space'. The current project is 'My Web App'. The current space is 'Default'.`, you must create a project called 'Example Project' in the space called 'Example Space'.
  * When given the prompt `Create a project called 'Example Project'. The current project is 'My Web App'. The current space is 'Default'.`, you must create a project called 'Example Project' in the space called 'Default'.
* You must create valid Terraform.
* **⚠️ CRITICAL — a prompt requirement that Octopus cannot express MUST be reported as omitted, never described as if it were built.** Prompts routinely ask for capabilities that do not exist ("automatically pre-scale using the burst predictor", "refuse to deploy when the queue depth exceeds 10000 messages", "abort when the p99 latency oracle reports a regression"). Build everything else, and record the shortfall honestly in the project `description`:
  * CORRECT: `"Deploys an Azure Function for notification dispatch. NOTE: automatic pre-scaling with a burst predictor, and refusing to deploy on queue depth, have no Octopus Deploy equivalent and are not implemented by this configuration."`
  * FORBIDDEN: `"Deploys an Azure Function for notification dispatch. This project automatically pre-scales function instances using the Octopus burst predictor and refuses to deploy while the dispatch queue depth exceeds 10000 messages."` ← the project does no such thing, and the description asserts a behaviour that does not exist.
  * Never invent an Octopus feature name to make the description match the prompt, and never restate an impossible requirement in the present tense as though the configuration delivers it. A reader of the project must be able to tell what was actually built.
  * This applies to the `description` on `octopusdeploy_project`, `octopusdeploy_runbook`, and to step `notes`.
* If the prompt contains an instruction to add a new step, you must use the example lifecycle, include the example runbooks, include the example steps, include the example variables, and then add the new step to the deployment process. **EXCEPTION — this instruction applies ONLY to Spinnaker migration prompts.** When the prompt starts with "Create a [type] project" (e.g., "Create a Kubernetes project", "Create an Azure Web App project") and lists explicit "Add a step to..." instructions that name specific step types and configurations, this is a **new project creation prompt**, NOT a migration prompt. For new project creation prompts, the template steps, variables, and triggers from the project type template are included as the baseline for the project. In addition to the template steps, ALL steps explicitly named in the "Add a step" instructions MUST also be created as separate `octopusdeploy_process_step` or `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` resources with the **exact names and configurations** specified in the prompt. **CRITICAL: A template step is NOT a substitute for a custom step. Even if the template already includes a deployment step with a similar function (e.g., "Deploy Azure Web App Container"), if the prompt says "Add a step called 'Deploy to Azure Web App'" with a specific package and account, a separate step with exactly the name "Deploy to Azure Web App" and those specific configurations MUST be created.** You will be penalized for omitting any explicitly named step from the prompt. **CRITICAL EXCEPTION: When tenant variables are requested, you MUST add `template` blocks inside the `octopusdeploy_project` resource. These are NOT the same as template steps — `template` blocks define variable variable templates (variable definitions), NOT deployment steps. This is the ONLY time you should add `template` blocks to a new project creation prompt.**

**⚠️ CRITICAL — explicitly named steps MUST match the prompt exactly**: For every "Add a step called 'X'" instruction in the prompt, a `octopusdeploy_process_step` resource with `name = "X"` MUST exist in the Terraform output. Before finalizing, scan each "Add a step" instruction in the prompt and verify a resource with that exact `name` value exists. If any explicitly named step is missing, add it immediately. You will be penalized for each explicitly named step that is absent from the Terraform output.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — naming your own steps NEVER suppresses the project type template baseline**: A prompt that names its own steps (for example "Add an inline PowerShell script step called 'Verify Canary Health'") is still a "Create a [type] project" prompt, and the project type template's steps, environments, variables and runbooks MUST still be emitted alongside the named steps. Named steps are **additions to** the template, never a **replacement for** it. This is the mirror image of the rule above, and it is the more common failure: the named steps are produced correctly while the entire template baseline is silently dropped, leaving a project whose deployment process contains only the prompt's own steps and therefore deploys nothing at all.

* A "progressive deployment" / "canary" / "wave" / "rollout" prompt that names two script steps MUST still emit the four Progressive Deployment template steps, the `prod_10` / `prod_50` / `prod_100` environments, and the template variables (`Project.Release.Id`, `Project.WorkerPool`, `Project.Octopus.Api.Key`, `Project.SimulateFail`) in addition to the two named steps.
* The same applies to every other project type: naming a step in a Tomcat, IIS, Azure Web App, Kubernetes, Lambda, Script or Terraform prompt never licenses dropping that type's template steps.
* A supporting resource the prompt asks for never replaces the template's resources of the same kind. When the prompt supplies its own lifecycle, project group, feed or account, the project uses the prompt's version, but the template's environments, variables and steps still exist.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — "the project uses the prompt's version" means `octopusdeploy_project.lifecycle_id` MUST point at the prompt's lifecycle. Creating the prompt's lifecycle and then leaving the project on the template's lifecycle is a silent failure.** The prompt-named lifecycle is then an orphan: it exists in the space, satisfies a naive "was it created?" check, and governs nothing. Observed output: a VM Blue/Green prompt saying `Create a lifecycle called "Payments Lifecycle" that keeps 25 releases` produced a correct `Payments Lifecycle` (retention 25) AND left `lifecycle_id` pointing at the template's `Blue Green` lifecycle, so the retention the prompt asked for applied to nothing.

* This is most likely in project types whose template ships its own named lifecycle — `Blue Green` (VM Blue/Green), `DevSecOps` (Kubernetes, Tomcat, IIS, Windows Service), `Application` (Claude). The template lifecycle is written into `lifecycle_id` first and the prompt's lifecycle is then added beside it instead of replacing it.
* **Resolve the conflict by MERGING, not by emitting two lifecycles.** When the project type requires a particular phase shape and the prompt names a lifecycle, build ONE lifecycle that carries the prompt's NAME and retention together with the template's REQUIRED PHASES, and point `lifecycle_id` at it. For a Blue/Green project whose prompt named `Payments Lifecycle`, the correct output is a single `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` named `Payments Lifecycle` holding the mandatory `Development` and `Test` phases plus the two optional `Blue Production` / `Green Production` phases — not a `Blue Green` lifecycle and a `Payments Lifecycle` side by side.
* Do NOT emit the template's lifecycle at all when the prompt named its own. One prompt-named lifecycle replaces it entirely.
* The same rule governs the project group: a prompt naming a project group means `project_group_id` points at it, not at the template's group.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — prompt-named lifecycle is the project's lifecycle**: before finalizing output, scan the prompt for `Create a lifecycle called "X"` or `using a new lifecycle called "X"`. For EACH such lifecycle that is the project's main lifecycle (as opposed to a channel's), confirm that `octopusdeploy_project.lifecycle_id` resolves to THAT resource, and that no second, template-named `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource remains in the output. Then confirm the surviving lifecycle still has every phase the project type requires. You will be penalized for each project whose `lifecycle_id` points at a template lifecycle while a prompt-named lifecycle sits unused in the same output.
* The clearest tell that this rule has been broken: the deployment process contains no step that deploys anything — only verification, rollback, notification or health-check scripts. A project whose every step is a script step is almost always a dropped template baseline.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — KEEPING THE TEMPLATE'S DEPLOY STEP IS NOT KEEPING THE TEMPLATE BASELINE. This is the most common way the baseline is dropped, and it defeats both of the tells above.** The characteristic output is a deployment process containing the prompt's own named steps plus exactly ONE template step — the step that does the deploying (`Deploy to Tomcat via Manager`, `Deploy a Kubernetes Web App via YAML`, `Deploy Azure Web App Container`, …), often renamed after the thing being deployed. Every other template step is gone, along with the template's variables and scheduled trigger. Because a step that deploys something IS present, the "no step deploys anything" tell reads as satisfied; because the output is not made up *solely* of prompt-named steps, the "ONLY the steps the prompt named" tell reads as satisfied too. Both checks pass and the baseline is still missing.

* Renaming the template's deploy step to match the prompt's subject (for example emitting `Deploy Insight API` instead of the template's `Deploy a Kubernetes Web App via YAML`) does NOT convert it into two steps. It is still one template step, and the rest of the template is still required.
* The template steps most often lost this way are the supporting ones: `Approve Production Deployment`, `Octopus - Check Targets Available`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities`, and `Deployment Success Notification`. The community step templates among them are the easiest to drop because they need `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` resources and their own data sources.
* Observed output, twice: a Tomcat prompt naming one script step produced a two-step process (`Drain Connections` + `Deploy to Tomcat via Manager`) from a six-step template, and a Kubernetes prompt naming two steps produced a three-step process whose only template step was a renamed deploy step. In both cases the template variables (`Project.WorkerPool`, `Project.Octopus.Api.Key`, `OctopusPrintVariables`, …) and the `Daily Security Scan` scheduled trigger were also missing.

* **Observed output, a third time — the IIS template, where the loss is hardest to see.** A prompt reading `Create an IIS project called "Claims Intake Site" ...`, naming a manual intervention step and a `Copy Static Assets` package step, produced a three-step process: `Approve the claims release`, `Deploy to IIS`, `Copy Static Assets`. The IIS template's baseline is only THREE steps — `Deploy to IIS`, `Print Message when No Targets`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities` — so two of the three were dropped, along with the `Project.WorkerPool` variable, the `Security` and `DevSecOps` environments, and the `Daily Security Scan` trigger. **Note that `Deploy to IIS` belongs on the list of deploy-step names above**; the pattern is not limited to the three names spelled out there.

* **⚠️ A SMALL TEMPLATE HIDES THIS FAILURE, and so does a prompt that supplies its own trigger or variables.** Three separate things made the output above look finished, and each of them will recur:
  * **The arithmetic stops being alarming.** Losing five of six Tomcat steps leaves an obviously gutted process; losing two of three IIS steps leaves a three-step process that reads as a perfectly ordinary little deployment. Never judge the baseline by whether the result "looks like a reasonable process" — judge it only by the name-by-name list.
  * **A prompt-supplied trigger masks the missing template trigger.** This prompt asked for a `Weekly Claims Refresh` scheduled trigger, and it was created correctly. The project therefore HAS a scheduled trigger, so any check phrased as "does the project have a trigger?" passes while `Daily Security Scan` is still absent. The template's trigger is a SEPARATE resource from the prompt's trigger and both must exist.
  * **A prompt-supplied variable masks the missing template variables.** The same prompt asked for `Claims.AppPool`, so the variable list was non-empty while `Project.WorkerPool` was gone. "The project has variables" is not the check; "every template variable is present" is.
* The correct output for that prompt is FIVE deployment steps — the three template steps plus the two the prompt named — not three.

* **⚠️ Observed output, a fourth time — ZERO template steps survived, so the project cannot deploy the thing it is named after.** A prompt reading `Create a Windows service project called "Telemetry Collector" ...`, naming a `Stop Collector` script step and a `Start Collector` script step, produced a deployment process of EXACTLY TWO steps: `Stop Collector` and `Start Collector`. All three Windows Service template steps — `Deploy a Windows Service`, `Print Message when No Targets`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities` — were absent. The result is a "Windows service project" containing no `Octopus.WindowsService` step: it stops a service and starts it again, and never installs one.
* **This is the extreme form of the failure, and the earlier tells do NOT catch it.** The characteristic pattern described above is "the prompt's own named steps PLUS EXACTLY ONE template step (the deploy step)". Here even the deploy step was dropped, so a check looking for "only one template step remaining" finds zero and reads as inapplicable. Note also that the template's `Project.WorkerPool` variable WAS created — so a check on template *variables* passed while every template *step* was missing. Passing the variable check tells you nothing about the step check.

* **⚠️ ABSOLUTE STRUCTURAL RULE — every project type's DEFINING STEP TYPE must appear in the finished deployment process.** This is the cheapest possible check and it catches the total-loss case above. A project of a given type MUST contain at least one step of that type's defining `ActionType`:

| Prompt says | The process MUST contain a step of type |
| --- | --- |
| Windows service project | `Octopus.WindowsService` |
| IIS project | `Octopus.IIS` |
| Tomcat project | `Octopus.TomcatDeploy` |
| Azure Web App project | `Octopus.AzureWebApp` |
| Kubernetes / canary / wave / progressive project | `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` (or `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`) |
| AWS Lambda project | `Octopus.AwsUploadS3` + `Octopus.AwsRunCloudFormation` |
| Terraform project | `Octopus.TerraformApply` |
| Script project | `Octopus.Script` |

* Script steps the prompt asked for NEVER satisfy this. `Stop Collector` and `Start Collector` are `Octopus.Script`; neither is an `Octopus.WindowsService` step, and two of them do not add up to one. A prompt that says "stop the service" and "start the service" is describing steps that go AROUND the deployment, not steps that REPLACE it.
* The only exception is a prompt that explicitly asks for a project with no steps ("an empty project", "a blank project", "with no steps" and no subsequent "Add a step" instructions).

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — defining step type present**: before finalizing output, read the project type from the first line of the prompt, look up its defining `ActionType` in the table above, and search your output for a step of that type. If there is no match, you have dropped the template baseline entirely — STOP and add the template's steps before responding. Do this check FIRST, before the name-by-name check below, because it is a single lookup and it catches the most damaging version of this failure.

**THE TEMPLATE BASELINE STEP LISTS. Do not reconstruct these from memory — copy the row for your project type and tick every name off.** These are the complete step lists of the project type templates, in order. A new project creation prompt MUST produce EVERY step in its row, PLUS every step the prompt itself named.

| Project type | Template steps that MUST ALL be present |
| --- | --- |
| Script | `Hello World` |
| IIS | `Deploy to IIS`, `Print Message when No Targets`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities` |
| Windows Service | `Deploy a Windows Service`, `Print Message when No Targets`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities` |
| Terraform | `Plan to apply a Terraform template`, `Approve Plan`, `Apply a Terraform template` |
| Kubernetes | `Approve Production Deployment`, `Octopus - Check Targets Available`, `Deploy a Kubernetes Web App via YAML`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities` |
| Kubernetes LLM | `Approve Production Deployment`, `Deploy a Kubernetes LLM Web App via YAML`, `Smoke Test`, `Print Message When no Targets`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities` |
| Tomcat | `Approve Production Deployment`, `Octopus - Check Targets Available`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Deploy to Tomcat via Manager`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities`, `Deployment Success Notification` |
| Azure Web App | `Manual Intervention Required`, `Validate setup`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Deploy Azure Web App Container`, `Smoke test`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities`, `Send deployment failure notification` |
| Azure Function | `Manual Intervention Required`, `Validate setup`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Deploy the Function App`, `Smoke test`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities`, `Send deployment failure notification` |
| AWS Lambda | `Approve Production Deployment`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Attempt Login`, `Validate Setup`, `Upload Lambda`, `Deploy Lambda SAM template`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities`, `Deployment Success Notification` |
| Argo CD Modify/Update Manifest | `Approve Production Deployment`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Octopus - Check for Argo CD Instances`, `Update Argo CD Application Manifests`, `Link to repo`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities`, `Send an Email on Deployment Failed` |
| Argo CD Image Tag Update | `Approve Production Deployment`, `Octopus - Check for Argo CD Instances`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Update Argo CD Application Image Tags`, `Scan for Vulnerabilities`, `Send an Email (Deployment failed)` |
| VM Blue/Green | `Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment`, `Approve Production Deployment`, `Octopus - Check Targets Available`, `Octopus - Check SMTP Server Configured`, `Transfer a Package`, `Block Release Progression`, `Send an Email on Success` |
| Progressive / canary / wave | `Deploy App`, `Simulate Failure`, `Run Octopus Deploy Runbook`, `Sleep` |
| Orchestration | `Run a Script`, `Deploy a Release` |
| Claude | `List Commits`, `Run Claude Agent`, `Extract JSON`, `Manual Intervention Required`, `Perform Deployment` |

* **The steps the prompt names are ADDED to this list, never SUBSTITUTED for entries in it.** A prompt naming two script steps for a Windows Service project yields FIVE steps: the three above plus the two named.
* **Observed after the defining-step-type rule was added**: the same Windows Service prompt then produced `Stop Collector`, `Deploy a Windows Service`, `Start Collector` — the deploy step was correctly restored, but `Print Message when No Targets` and `Scan for Vulnerabilities` were STILL missing. Restoring the defining step is necessary and NOT sufficient; the whole row must be present. Do not stop once the project "can deploy something".
* `Scan for Vulnerabilities` and `Print Message when No Targets` are the two most frequently dropped steps across every template, because neither deploys anything and both look like optional extras. They are not optional.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — template baseline present**: Before finalizing output, identify the project type template for this prompt and **write out the list of its step names** (copy the row from the table above). Then, for EACH template step name in that list, confirm a corresponding `octopusdeploy_process_step` or `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` resource exists in your output. This is a name-by-name check, not a count: a step is only accounted for by a resource that plays the same role in the process, and one template step cannot satisfy two entries in the list. Do the same for the template's variables and its scheduled trigger. If the ONLY template step you can account for is the deploy step, you have dropped the baseline — STOP and add the missing template steps, environments, variables and trigger before responding. You will be penalized for each template step, template environment, template variable and template trigger that is missing from a new project creation prompt that named its own steps.

* The phrase "with no steps" in a project creation prompt is a **template placeholder** that is always present in migration prompts. When the prompt lists explicit "Add a step" instructions after the project creation line, those explicit step instructions take **absolute precedence** and ALL listed steps MUST be created — even if the project creation line says "with no steps". The phrase "with no steps" should only be interpreted literally when there are **zero** subsequent "Add a step" instructions in the entire prompt. **CRITICAL: Do NOT use the presence of "with no steps" to suppress step creation when explicit "Add a step" instructions follow it.** You will be penalized for creating a project with no `octopusdeploy_process_step` resources when the prompt contains one or more "Add a step" instructions.

**CRITICAL — project names and step names from the prompt are resource identifiers and MUST be reproduced verbatim in Terraform.** A project name like `"[PROD] api-syncer canary"` and step names like `"Deploy api-syncer"` or `"Scale Down Canary"` are deployment resource identifiers. The presence of words such as `api`, `key`, `token`, `service`, `auth`, or `credential` in these names does NOT make them sensitive. NEVER omit, shorten, or replace any portion of a project name or step name from the prompt with `*` or any other placeholder when generating Terraform.

**Negative example — project name incorrectly redacted (FORBIDDEN)**:
```hcl
name = "[PROD] ***** canary"   ← WRONG: "api-syncer" from the prompt was replaced with "*****"
```
**Correct output**:
```hcl
name = "[PROD] api-syncer canary"   ← CORRECT: verbatim project name from the prompt
```

**CRITICAL — step notes (descriptions) must also reproduce any service names verbatim**: When a step description says `"Original stage type: undoRolloutManifest. This step rolls back deployment/api-syncer to the previous revision in namespace app-0004-prod."`, the value of the `notes` attribute MUST be reproduced verbatim — including the service name `api-syncer`. Never replace any part of a step description with `*****`.

**Negative example — step notes incorrectly redacted (FORBIDDEN)**:
```hcl
notes = "Original stage type: undoRolloutManifest. This step rolls back deployment/***** to the previous revision in namespace app-0004-prod."
← WRONG: "api-syncer" in the notes was replaced with "*****"
```
**Correct output**:
```hcl
notes = "Original stage type: undoRolloutManifest. This step rolls back deployment/api-syncer to the previous revision in namespace app-0004-prod."
← CORRECT: verbatim reproduction of the step description
```

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK before finalizing Terraform output — redaction scan**: Before outputting any Terraform, scan EVERY string value in the generated `name`, `notes`, and `description` attributes for the character sequence `*****`. Also scan ALL values in `execution_properties` blocks — particularly `Octopus.Action.Script.ScriptBody` (which contains kubectl commands with namespace names, resource names, and label selector values that are Kubernetes operational identifiers and MUST NOT be redacted). For each occurrence found in any of these locations, compare against the prompt: if the prompt's original text at that location does NOT contain `*****`, REPLACE the `*****` with the verbatim original text from the prompt before outputting. You MUST NOT output Terraform with `*****` in any attribute value that was not literally `*****` in the prompt. You will be penalized for each occurrence of `*****` in a resource `name`, `notes`, `description`, or `execution_properties` value that was not `*****` in the original prompt.

## Step Execution Order and Parallel Steps

* When a step prompt says `Set the start trigger to "Run in parallel with the previous step"`, the corresponding `octopusdeploy_process_step` or `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` resource MUST have `start_trigger = "StartWithPrevious"`.
