* You will be penalized for setting "is_optional_phase" to false when the example configuration sets it to true.

**CRITICAL — lifecycles with parallel production stacks**: A lifecycle that models parallel production stacks, for example a blue/green lifecycle with "Blue Production" and "Green Production" environments, MUST mark the phase for each stack with `is_optional_phase = true`. Each stack is deployed to independently, so a release must be able to be promoted to one stack without being deployed to the other. Marking either stack's phase as mandatory forces every release to be deployed to both stacks, which defeats the purpose of a blue/green lifecycle.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — a lifecycle MUST contain at least one phase that is NOT optional.** Octopus rejects a lifecycle whose every phase sets `is_optional_phase = true` with `There was a problem with your request. [Lifecycle 'X' must have at least one phase that is not optional.]`, the Terraform apply aborts, and **no project is created at all**. This constraint interacts directly with the blue/green rule above and is the single easiest way to produce an all-optional lifecycle by accident:

* A blue/green prompt that asks only for "a lifecycle containing the production environments" would, following the rule above alone, yield a lifecycle whose ONLY phases are the two optional parallel stacks — which Octopus refuses to create.
* The fix is to keep a mandatory pre-production phase. Emit a first phase (for example `Development`, or `Development` and `Test`) with `is_optional_phase = false`, followed by the optional blue/green stack phases. Add that pre-production phase even when the prompt mentions only production environments — a lifecycle needs somewhere for a release to start.
* Only if the lifecycle genuinely cannot have a pre-production phase should you mark exactly ONE stack phase non-optional. Prefer adding the pre-production phase, because marking a stack mandatory partially defeats the blue/green alternation.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — this constraint applies to EVERY lifecycle in the output, and the one that actually breaks is usually the SECOND lifecycle, not the blue/green one.** The rule above is easy to read as a blue/green rule and apply only to the project's main lifecycle. The lifecycle that fails in practice is a secondary lifecycle the prompt named without describing its phases — most often the lifecycle behind a channel, as in `Add a channel called "Hotfix Channel" using a new lifecycle called "Gateway Hotfix"`. Because the prompt says nothing about that lifecycle's phases, its phases get invented, and in a blue/green project they get invented by copying the two optional production stack phases. The result is an all-optional lifecycle and this error:

```
Error: unable to create lifecycle

  with octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_gateway_hotfix[0],

Octopus API error: There was a problem with your request. [Lifecycle 'Gateway Hotfix' must have at least one phase that is not optional. If you want to disable all progression rules then please follow these instructions:]
```

* This has been observed: a VM Blue/Green prompt that asked for a `Hotfix Channel` backed by a new `Gateway Hotfix` lifecycle produced a valid main lifecycle and an all-optional hotfix lifecycle. The apply aborted partway through, so the channel was never created and the project was left incomplete — with steps but no channel and no hotfix lifecycle.
* **When the prompt names a lifecycle but does not describe its phases, do NOT copy the phase shape of another lifecycle.** Give it a straightforward mandatory progression instead: one `phase` per environment in the project's environment list, each with `is_optional_phase = false`. A hotfix lifecycle is a fast path to production, not a parallel-stack lifecycle, so `is_optional_phase = true` has no reason to appear in it at all.
* `is_optional_phase = true` is justified ONLY by a specific reason in the prompt or the project type template — parallel production stacks (blue/green), or a phase the prompt explicitly calls optional. Absent such a reason, write `is_optional_phase = false`.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — at least one mandatory phase**: Before finalizing output, list EVERY `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource you have written — the project's lifecycle, every channel's lifecycle, and any additional lifecycle the prompt named. Walk that list one resource at a time; do not check only the first one. For EACH lifecycle, count its `phase` blocks and count how many set `is_optional_phase = true`. If those two numbers are EQUAL, every phase is optional and the apply will fail — set `is_optional_phase = false` on the appropriate phase, or add a non-optional pre-production phase, before responding. You will be penalized for every lifecycle in which all phases are optional.

**Example — blue/green lifecycle production phases (CORRECT)**:
```hcl
phase {
  automatic_deployment_targets          = []
  optional_deployment_targets           = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_blue_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_blue_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_blue_production[0].id}"]
  name                                  = "Blue Production"
  is_optional_phase                     = true
  minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0
}
phase {
  automatic_deployment_targets          = []
  optional_deployment_targets           = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_green_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_green_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_green_production[0].id}"]
  name                                  = "Green Production"
  is_optional_phase                     = true
  minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0
}
```

**Negative example — blue/green production phases without `is_optional_phase` (FORBIDDEN)**:
```hcl
phase {
  automatic_deployment_targets          = []
  optional_deployment_targets           = ["${...environment_blue_production...}"]
  name                                  = "Blue Production"
  minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0
}  ← WRONG: "is_optional_phase" is missing, so the phase defaults to mandatory
```

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — optional phase verification**: Before finalizing any `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource, check:
1. Does the example configuration set `is_optional_phase = true` on any phase? If YES → does every one of those phases set `is_optional_phase = true` in your response?
2. Does the lifecycle model parallel production stacks such as blue/green? If YES → is the phase for each stack marked `is_optional_phase = true`?

## Lifecycle Phase `minimum_environments_before_promotion` Instructions

**⚠️ CRITICAL — when the prompt gives a promotion threshold that IS valid, you MUST set `minimum_environments_before_promotion` to it; do not silently drop it.** Clamping (below) applies only when the requested number is larger than the phase's environment count. When the number is less than or equal to that count it is legal and MUST appear on the `phase` block. All of these set it explicitly:

* "where a release may be promoted after 2 environments in the phase have been deployed to" on a phase with 2 environments → `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 2`
* "releases must be promoted only after 1 environment has been deployed to" → `= 1`
* "a release may be promoted once N of this phase's environments have been deployed to" → `= N`, clamped to the phase's environment count

**⚠️ CRITICAL — "after N successful deployments" is NOT a promotion threshold and MUST NOT be copied into this attribute.** Only a phrase that counts *environments inside this phase* sets `minimum_environments_before_promotion`. A phrase that counts *deployments*, *releases*, or *successful runs* describes an Octopus feature that does not exist, and MUST be ignored entirely with the attribute left at `0`. See the rule below, which is authoritative and overrides any reading of the bullets above. Emitting `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 2` for "automatically promote releases after 2 successful deployments" on a phase holding one environment makes the Octopus API reject the whole lifecycle with `The phase "X" requires a minimum of 2 environments for promotion, but only 1 environments have been added`, the Terraform apply aborts, and **no project is created at all** — the single worst outcome, since every other resource in the prompt is lost with it.

Leaving it at `0` (or omitting it, which defaults to `0`) when the prompt asked for a threshold silently changes the lifecycle's behaviour: every release becomes promotable immediately. You will be penalized for each phase whose prompt stated a threshold and whose `minimum_environments_before_promotion` is `0`.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — promotion thresholds**: before finalizing output, re-read each phase description in the prompt and apply these two steps IN ORDER to every number it mentions. Do not skip step 1; a number that fails it is discarded and never reaches step 2.

1. **What does the number count?** If it counts *deployments*, *releases*, *successful runs*, *promotions*, or deployments *to a DIFFERENT phase's environment* — for example "automatically promotes releases after 2 successful deployments to Development" — it is NOT a promotion threshold. Set `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0` and discard the number. ONLY a number that counts environments *inside the phase being described* survives to step 2.
2. **Is the surviving number within bounds?** Count the environment entries in that same `phase` block (`automatic_deployment_targets` + `optional_deployment_targets`). If the number is greater than that count, write `0`, not the number. "Clamped" is an instruction you MUST carry out, not a caveat — a phase holding one environment can only ever be written `0` or `1`, whatever the prompt says.

Note that the "omit it if unsure" guidance elsewhere in this file applies ONLY to `release_retention_with_strategy` / `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` — it does NOT license dropping `minimum_environments_before_promotion`, `is_optional_phase`, or any other phase attribute, and it does NOT license dropping a certificate the prompt asked for.

**⚠️ Observed failure — a phase threshold phrased as deployments to the PREVIOUS phase's environment.** The prompt `Create a lifecycle called "Insight Lifecycle" with phases "Development", "Test", and "Production", where the "Test" phase automatically promotes releases after 2 successful deployments to "Development"` produced `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 2` on the one-environment "Test" phase. The apply aborted with `The phase "Test" requires a minimum of 2 environments for promotion, but only 1 environments have been added`, and the entire project — steps, variables, tenants, runbook, target — was lost, leaving only the orphaned feeds, tenants and accounts that had already been created. This phrasing is doubly disqualified: the number counts deployments rather than environments, AND the environment it names ("Development") is not in the phase the threshold is written on. Attaching the number to the phase named LAST in the sentence is the specific mistake to avoid — the correct output is `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0` on every phase of that lifecycle.

**CRITICAL — `minimum_environments_before_promotion` MUST NOT exceed the number of environments in the same `phase` block**: The `minimum_environments_before_promotion` attribute counts environments *within its own phase* that must be deployed to before a release can be promoted to the next phase. It does NOT refer to environments in earlier phases, to a number of deployments, or to a number of successful releases. Octopus rejects a lifecycle where the value is greater than the number of entries in that phase's `automatic_deployment_targets` plus `optional_deployment_targets` arrays, and the apply fails with:

```
Octopus API error: There was a problem with your request. [The phase "Staging"
requires a minimum of 3 environments for promotion, but only 1 environments have
been added. Please add more environments or reduce the minimum required.]
```

* You MUST set `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0` on every phase unless the prompt explicitly states how many environments *inside that phase* must be deployed to before promotion. `0` means "all environments in the phase" and is always valid.
* When a phase contains exactly one environment — which is the case for almost every phase — the ONLY valid values are `0` and `1`. You will be penalized for any other value.
* When a prompt asks to promote "after N successful deployments", "after N successful releases", "once N deployments have completed", or similar, this is NOT `minimum_environments_before_promotion`. Octopus has no such feature. Set `minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0` and ignore the N entirely. You will be penalized for copying N from the prompt into `minimum_environments_before_promotion`.
* A prompt phrase that names a *different* phase, such as "promote to Staging after 3 successful deployments to Test", never sets a value on the phase being promoted to, and never sets a value greater than the environment count of the phase it names.

**Negative example — `minimum_environments_before_promotion` greater than the phase environment count (FORBIDDEN)**:
```hcl
# Prompt says: The "Staging" phase must automatically promote releases after 3 successful deployments to "Test"
phase {
  automatic_deployment_targets          = []
  optional_deployment_targets           = ["${...environment_staging...}"]
  name                                  = "Staging"
  is_optional_phase                     = true
  minimum_environments_before_promotion = 3
}  ← WRONG: the phase has 1 environment, so the apply fails
```

**Correct example — same prompt, valid phase**:
```hcl
phase {
  automatic_deployment_targets          = []
  optional_deployment_targets           = ["${...environment_staging...}"]
  name                                  = "Staging"
  is_optional_phase                     = true
  minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0
}
```

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — `minimum_environments_before_promotion` bounds**: Before finalizing any `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource, for EACH `phase` block count the total number of environment entries across `automatic_deployment_targets` and `optional_deployment_targets`. If `minimum_environments_before_promotion` is greater than that count, change it to `0`. You will be penalized for each phase where the value exceeds the phase's environment count.

## Retention Policy for New Project Creation Prompts

**CRITICAL — when a new project creation prompt specifies a retention policy (e.g., "with a retention policy of 30 days for releases" or "Set the retention policy to 5 items")**:

1. If the project uses the **Default Lifecycle** (looked up via data source only, NOT a resource), the retention policy from the prompt CANNOT be applied — the Default Lifecycle cannot be modified by Terraform. In this case, **ignore the retention policy instruction entirely** and do NOT add any `release_retention_with_strategy` or `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` blocks.

2. If the project uses a **custom lifecycle being created** as a new `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource, apply the retention policy to the lifecycle resource's `release_retention_with_strategy` block. The `unit` attribute MUST match what the prompt specifies:
   - When the prompt says "N days" → use `unit = "Days"` and `quantity_to_keep = N`
   - When the prompt says "N items" or "keep N releases" → use `unit = "Items"` and `quantity_to_keep = N`

**⚠️ CRITICAL — "the project uses a custom lifecycle" includes the TEMPLATE-DEFAULT lifecycles, and includes lifecycles shared by several projects in the same prompt.** The only lifecycle exempt from a retention instruction is the Default Lifecycle, because it is looked up through a data source and cannot be modified. Every other lifecycle — `Application`, `DevSecOps`, `Progressive`, `Blue Green`, or any lifecycle the prompt named — IS emitted as an `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource, so a retention instruction MUST be written onto it.

* **Observed failure**: a multi-project prompt created "Cart Service", "Search Service" and an orchestration project "Retail Release Train" all bound to the template's `Application` lifecycle, and asked for the orchestration project's retention policy to keep 10 releases. The lifecycle was emitted with the template's default `30 Days` / `30 Days` and the instruction was silently dropped.
* Octopus retention is a property of the LIFECYCLE, not of the project, and `retention_policy` on `octopusdeploy_project` is forbidden. So when several projects in one prompt share a lifecycle and the prompt asks for retention on one of them, write the requested policy onto that shared lifecycle. Applying it once is correct — do not create a duplicate lifecycle, and do not drop the instruction because the lifecycle is shared.
* Only when the project's lifecycle is the Default Lifecycle is dropping the retention instruction correct.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — "the project's lifecycle" means the lifecycle in `octopusdeploy_project.lifecycle_id`, NOT a channel's lifecycle.** When the output contains more than one `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource — which is the norm as soon as the prompt adds a channel — the retention instruction belongs on the one the PROJECT resource points at. Putting it on a channel's lifecycle leaves the project itself running the template default, so the instruction is effectively lost.

* **Observed failure**: a Lambda prompt said `Set the retention policy of the project's lifecycle to keep 17 releases and 34 days of files` and also asked for `a channel called "HighRes" that uses a new lifecycle called "HighRes Lifecycle"`. The project was bound to the template's `DevSecOps` lifecycle, but `17 Items` / `34 Days` was written onto `HighRes Lifecycle`, and `DevSecOps` was given an invented `5 Days` / `5 Days`.
* **Do NOT invent retention values for a lifecycle the prompt said nothing about.** A lifecycle with no retention instruction keeps the values from the example configuration it came from. Inventing `5 Days` or any other figure silently changes behaviour the prompt never asked to change.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — retention lands on the right lifecycle**: before finalizing output, read `lifecycle_id` on the `octopusdeploy_project` resource and note which `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` it names. Confirm the prompt's retention figures appear on THAT lifecycle. Then check every other `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` in the output and confirm its retention blocks match the example configuration rather than a value you chose. You will be penalized for writing the retention policy onto a lifecycle other than the project's, and for inventing retention figures the prompt did not give.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — do NOT default to 30 Days when the prompt specifies items**: If the prompt says "Set the retention policy to 5 items", the lifecycle MUST use `quantity_to_keep = 5` and `unit = "Items"`. Setting `quantity_to_keep = 30` and `unit = "Days"` is WRONG — it ignores the prompt instruction.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — a two-part retention prompt sets the two policies INDEPENDENTLY**: A prompt of the form "retain N releases and M days of files", "keep N releases and M days of packages", or "retention of N releases and M days" describes TWO separate policies with TWO different units, and each must be resolved on its own:

* "N releases" (or "N items") is the **release** retention: `release_retention_with_strategy` with `strategy = "Count"`, `quantity_to_keep = N` and `unit = "Items"`.
* "M days of files" (or "of packages", "of artifacts") is the **tentacle/file** retention: `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` with `strategy = "Count"`, `quantity_to_keep = M` and `unit = "Days"`.

Do NOT apply one unit to both policies. The mistake is easiest to make when N and M happen to be the same number — for example "keep 7 releases and 7 days of files" MUST produce release retention `7 Items` and tentacle retention `7 Days`, never `7 Days` for both. The word "releases" always means `Items`; only an explicit "days" means `Days`.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — retention policy verification**: Before finalizing any `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource, check:
1. Did the prompt specify a retention policy? If YES → did you apply the correct `quantity_to_keep` and `unit` values?
2. Did the prompt say "items"? If YES → is `unit = "Items"` (NOT `"Days"`)?
3. Did the prompt say "days"? If YES → is `unit = "Days"` (NOT `"Items"`)?
4. Did the prompt give TWO figures (one for releases, one for files/days)? If YES → does the release policy use `Items` with the releases figure, and the tentacle policy use `Days` with the days figure? Confirm the two policies do not share a unit that only one of them was given.
5. Does every `release_retention_with_strategy` and `tentacle_retention_with_strategy` block include `strategy = "Count"`? This attribute is required.

You will be penalized for using `unit = "Days"` when the prompt says "items", for using `unit = "Items"` when the prompt says "days", and for copying one policy's unit onto the other in a two-part retention prompt.

A useful tell that the lifecycle was not written in the canonical shape is the absence of `count`: the vertex in the error above is `octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_search_lifecycle` with no `[0]` index, whereas a correctly generated lifecycle always appears as `...[0]`.

**This is the canonical `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` shape. Follow it exactly.**

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "lifecycle_my_lifecycle" {
  partial_name = "My Lifecycle"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_my_lifecycle" {
  count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_my_lifecycle.lifecycles) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name  = "My Lifecycle"

  release_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }

  tentacle_retention_with_strategy {
    strategy         = "Count"
    quantity_to_keep = 30
    unit             = "Days"
  }

  phase {
    name                                  = "Dev"
    optional_deployment_targets            = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_development[0].id}"]
    automatic_deployment_targets           = []
    is_optional_phase                      = false
    minimum_environments_before_promotion  = 0
  }

  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

3. **NEVER add a `retention_policy` block to an `octopusdeploy_project` resource** — this is always forbidden regardless of prompt content.

**Lifecycle Selection for New Project Creation Prompts**:

* When the prompt says "Use the Default Lifecycle" — use ONLY `data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_default_lifecycle.lifecycles[0].id`. Do NOT create an `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource.
* When the prompt says "Use the [Name] lifecycle" for any non-Default lifecycle — create BOTH a `data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles"` data source AND an `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resource for that lifecycle name.
* When the lifecycle name in the prompt does not match "Default Lifecycle", create the lifecycle with phases corresponding to the environments mentioned in the prompt (e.g., "Development", "Test", "Production").

**CRITICAL — when the prompt specifies the Default Lifecycle, the template's default lifecycle resource MUST NOT be created**: Each project type template includes a default lifecycle resource (e.g., `lifecycle_application` for Terraform templates, `lifecycle_devsecops` for Kubernetes templates). When the prompt says "Use the Default Lifecycle", these template-default lifecycle resources MUST NOT be created as `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resources. Use ONLY the `data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_default_lifecycle` data source for the project's `lifecycle_id`.

**Negative example — creating a template-default lifecycle when prompt says "Use the Default Lifecycle" (FORBIDDEN)**:
```hcl
# Prompt says: Use the "Default Lifecycle" lifecycle
# WRONG: template's default lifecycle resource is created even though Default Lifecycle was requested
resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_application" { ... }  ← WRONG: must not be created
resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_my_terraform_project" {
  lifecycle_id = "${octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_application[0].id}"  ← WRONG: must use Default Lifecycle
}
```

**Correct output** — only the Default Lifecycle data source is used, no lifecycle resource is created:
```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "lifecycle_default_lifecycle" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Default Lifecycle"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_my_terraform_project" {
  lifecycle_id = "${data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_default_lifecycle.lifecycles[0].id}"
}
```

You will be penalized for creating `octopusdeploy_lifecycle` resources (e.g., `lifecycle_application`, `lifecycle_devsecops`) when the prompt says to use the Default Lifecycle.

**Example — prompt says "Use the Security lifecycle"**:
```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "lifecycle_security" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Security"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_lifecycle" "lifecycle_security" {
  count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_security.lifecycles) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name  = "Security"
  phase {
    name                         = "Development"
    optional_deployment_targets  = ["${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_development[0].id}"]
    automatic_deployment_targets = []
    minimum_environments_before_promotion = 0
    is_optional_phase = false
  }
}
```

## Data Resources Instructions

* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_projects" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.<data name>.project) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_feeds" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.<data name>.feeds) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_worker_pools" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_worker_pools.<data name>.worker_pools) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_environments" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.<data name>.environments) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_tag_sets" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.<data name>.tag_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.<data name>.lifecycles) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_certificates" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.<data name>.certificates) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_git_credentials" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_git_credentials.<data name>.git_credentials) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_accounts" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.<data name>.accounts) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_machine_policies" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_machine_policies.<data name>.machine_policies) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_channels" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_channels.<data name>.channels) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* When getting the length of the "data" "octopusdeploy_deployment_targets" resource, the format is "${length(data.octopusdeploy_deployment_targets.<data name>.deployment_targets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}".
* The "<data name>" in the above examples must be replaced with the name of the data resource included in the output.
* You will be penalized for using a "<data name>" that does not match the name of the data resource included in the output.

## Account Data Instructions

* The "account_type" in a "octopusdeploy_accounts" data source must be set to one of the following, matching the case exactly:
  * "AmazonWebServicesAccount"
  * "AmazonWebServicesRoleAccount"
  * "AmazonWebServicesOidcAccount"
  * "AzureServicePrincipal"
  * "AzureOIDC"
  * "AzureSubscription"
  * "GenericOidcAccount"
  * "GoogleCloudAccount"
  * "None"
  * "SshKeyPair"
  * "Token"
  * "UsernamePassword"

* This is an example of a "octopusdeploy_accounts" data source with a correctly defined "account_type" attribute:

```
data "octopusdeploy_accounts" "account_azure" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Azure"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
  account_type = "AzureOIDC"
}
```

* You will be penalized for setting the "account_type" to an empty value.

## Certificate Creation for New Project Creation Prompts

When creating a `octopusdeploy_certificate` resource, use placeholder values for the `certificate_data` and `password` fields. Ignore any details in the prompt to set these values to real certificate data.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — if the prompt asks for a certificate, you MUST create the `octopusdeploy_certificate` resource. Omitting it is NEVER acceptable.** A prompt that merely names a certificate ("create a certificate called 'Ledger Signing Cert'") supplies everything you need, because the data and password are placeholders that are substituted after generation — you do NOT need real certificate data from the prompt, and "the prompt did not supply certificate data" is NEVER a valid reason to skip the resource. Certificates are safe to create; there is no failure mode to avoid by leaving one out.

**A `Certificate`-typed variable MUST point at a certificate you actually created.** The `value` of a `Certificate`-typed `octopusdeploy_variable` must resolve to a certificate ID, written in exactly this form:

```hcl
value = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_my_cert.certificates) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_my_cert.certificates[0].id : octopusdeploy_certificate.certificate_my_cert[0].id}"
```

* NEVER emit a `Certificate`-typed variable with `value = ""` or with no `value` at all. An empty value creates a variable that silently references nothing, which is worse than either creating the certificate properly or omitting both — the project looks correct in the export but the certificate slot is blank at deployment time.
* If for any reason you do not create the certificate, you MUST also drop the `Certificate`-typed variable that referenced it, and any step property pointing at it. A dangling reference is never acceptable.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — certificate requested**: if the prompt names a certificate, confirm your output contains BOTH an `octopusdeploy_certificate` resource for it AND (when the prompt asked for a certificate variable) a `Certificate`-typed variable whose `value` uses the ternary form above. You will be penalized for a certificate named in the prompt that is missing from the output, and penalized for a `Certificate`-typed variable with an empty value.

**CRITICAL — NEVER use real certificate data for `certificate_data`, and NEVER use a real password for `password`.** Always use placeholder values: `certificate_data = "placeholder"` and `password = "Change Me!"`. The `certificate_data` and `password` fields are replaced in a post-processing step, so the fields simply need to exist with placeholder values, but the values will not be used.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — `certificate_data` and `password` MUST each be present as a plain attribute line holding a plain string, and MUST NOT be a `${var....}` reference.** The certificate values are substituted with known-good fixture values after generation, but that substitution only works on a literal attribute line inside a top-level `octopusdeploy_certificate` resource. So:

* The resource MUST be written at the top level, with `resource "octopusdeploy_certificate" "..." {` starting at the beginning of the line and the resource's closing `}` on its own line with no indentation.
* Both `certificate_data = "..."` and `password = "..."` MUST appear as their own attribute lines inside that resource. NEVER omit either attribute — an `octopusdeploy_certificate` with no `certificate_data` line cannot be fixed up and always fails to parse.
* NEVER write `certificate_data = "${var.something}"` or `password = "${var.something}"`, and never move the value into a `variable` block, a `local`, or a nested block. A variable reference resolves to an empty string when the variable has no value, and an empty certificate blob produces exactly the "Unable to parse certificate" failure above. When you have no example blob to copy, write a short placeholder string such as `certificate_data = "placeholder"` and `password = "Change Me!"` on their own lines — the placeholder will be replaced with a valid certificate, whereas a variable reference will not be.
* A certificate that fails to apply does not fail alone: every resource ordered after it — the certificate-typed variable that references it, tenants, tag sets — is abandoned, and the retry that follows usually drops them from the project entirely. Getting this shape right is what keeps the rest of the prompt's resources.

**This is the ONLY shape an `octopusdeploy_certificate` resource may take.** Copy it exactly, changing only the resource label, the `name`, and the data source names:

```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_certificates" "certificate_my_cert" {
  partial_name = "My Cert"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

resource "octopusdeploy_certificate" "certificate_my_cert" {
  count            = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_my_cert.certificates) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name             = "My Cert"
  certificate_data = "placeholder"
  password         = "Change Me!"
  environments     = []
  tenant_tags      = []
  tenants          = []
  lifecycle {
    ignore_changes  = [certificate_data, password]
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
  depends_on = []
}
```

Note that `certificate_data` and `password` are single-line string attributes at the top level of the resource. Do not wrap them in a nested block, do not use a heredoc, do not spread the value across lines, and do not move them into a `variable`. Any deviation from this shape prevents the fixture substitution and the apply fails with "Unable to parse certificate".

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — certificates**: for every `octopusdeploy_certificate` resource in your output, confirm the resource line is unindented, confirm a `certificate_data` line and a `password` line are both present as single-line string attributes, and confirm neither contains `${var.`. This check applies to EVERY project type, including IIS, Tomcat, Azure Function, and Windows Service projects whose example configuration contains no certificate at all — having no example to copy is exactly when you must fall back to the shape above.

## Binding an Octopus Certificate to an IIS HTTPS Site

**⚠️ CRITICAL — an HTTPS binding consumes a certificate through `certificateVariable`, NEVER through `thumbprint`.** The two fields inside an `Octopus.Action.IISWebSite.Bindings` entry are not interchangeable:

* `certificateVariable` holds the NAME of a project variable of type `Certificate` — a plain string such as `"Project.Certificate"`. This is the ONLY way to bind a certificate that Octopus manages.
* `thumbprint` holds the SHA-1 thumbprint of a certificate already installed in the machine's certificate store. It is a hex string, and Octopus does not resolve it against the certificate library.

**Observed output.** A prompt saying `Create a certificate called "Claims TLS" and use it in the IIS deploy step to bind the HTTPS site` produced a binding of `{ "protocol" = "https", "port" = "443", "certificateVariable" = null, "thumbprint" = "Certificates-588" }`. The apply succeeded and the result is broken three ways at once:

1. `Certificates-588` is a certificate ID, not a thumbprint. At deployment time IIS is asked for a certificate whose thumbprint is literally the text `Certificates-588`, which no store contains, so the binding fails.
2. The ID is hardcoded, so the configuration is not portable — re-applied against any other space it points at a different certificate or at nothing.
3. Because the only reference to the certificate is a string buried inside a `jsonencode` blob, the project has NO link to the certificate resource, so the certificate is not exported with the project. The prompt's certificate silently disappears from the project.

The template only ever shows an `http` binding with `thumbprint = null`, so there is no example to copy from — that absence is exactly why `thumbprint` gets filled in with whatever certificate reference is at hand. Emit ALL THREE pieces instead:

```hcl
# 1. the certificate itself (see the canonical shape above)
resource "octopusdeploy_certificate" "certificate_claims_tls" { ... }

# 2. a Certificate-typed project variable pointing at it
resource "octopusdeploy_variable" "project_certificate_1" {
  count    = "..."
  owner_id = "..."
  name     = "Project.Certificate"
  type     = "Certificate"
  value    = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_claims_tls.certificates) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_certificates.certificate_claims_tls.certificates[0].id : octopusdeploy_certificate.certificate_claims_tls[0].id}"
}
```
```hcl
# 3. the binding, naming that variable and leaving thumbprint null
"Octopus.Action.IISWebSite.Bindings" = jsonencode([
  {
    "certificateVariable" = "Project.Certificate"
    "requireSni"          = "False"
    "enabled"             = "True"
    "protocol"            = "https"
    "port"                = "443"
    "host"                = ""
    "thumbprint"          = null
  },
])
```

* Keep the template's existing `http` binding entry as well unless the prompt says to remove it; add the `https` entry alongside it.
* `Octopus.Action.EnabledFeatures` does not need a certificate feature — the binding is part of `Octopus.Features.IISWebSite`.
* The same rule holds for any step property that names a certificate: reference a `Certificate`-typed variable by name, never a `Certificates-NNN` id and never a resource id interpolation inside a JSON string.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — HTTPS bindings**: search your output for `"thumbprint"`. Every occurrence MUST be `null`. If any holds a value, and especially if that value starts with `Certificates-`, move the certificate to a `Certificate`-typed variable and put that variable's NAME in `certificateVariable` instead. Then confirm that every certificate named in the prompt is referenced by at least one `Certificate`-typed variable — a certificate reachable only from inside a `jsonencode` blob is an orphaned certificate.

## Data and Resource pairing instructions

When a “data” resource is referenced in a resource’s attributes, the data resource must be included in the output.

For example, when the “lifecycle_id” attribute is set to:

```
"${length(data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_<data ame>.lifecycles) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_<data name>.lifecycles[0].id : octopusdeploy_lifecycle.lifecycle_<name>[0].id}"
```

Then the following "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" data resource must be included in the output:

```
data "octopusdeploy_lifecycles" "lifecycle_<data name>" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "<Name>"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

When the "project_group_id" attribute is set to:

```
"${length(data.octopusdeploy_project_groups.project_group_<data name>.project_groups) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_project_groups.project_group_<data name>.project_groups[0].id : octopusdeploy_project_group.project_group_<name>[0].id}"
```

Then the following "octopusdeploy_project_groups" data resource must be included in the output:

```
data "octopusdeploy_project_groups" "project_group_<data name>" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "<Name>"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

And the following "octopusdeploy_project_group" resource must be defined:

```
resource "octopusdeploy_project_group" "project_group_<name>" {
  count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_project_groups.project_group_<name>.project_groups) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name  = "<Name>"
  ...
}
```

When the "feed_id" attribute is set to:

```
"${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_docker_hub.feeds) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_docker_hub.feeds[0].id : octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry.feed_docker_hub[0].id}"
```

Then the following "octopusdeploy_feeds" data resource must be included in the output:

```
data "octopusdeploy_feeds" "<name>" {
  feed_type    = "Docker"
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "<Name>"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

And the following "octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry" resource must be defined:

```
resource "octopusdeploy_docker_container_registry" "<name>" {
  count                                = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_feeds.feed_docker_hub.feeds) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                                 = "<Name>"
  # Populate the rest of the feed attributes here
}
```

When the "environment_id" attribute is set to:

```
"${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_<data name>.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_<data name>.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_<name>[0].id}"
```

Then the following "octopusdeploy_environments" data resource must be included in the output:

```
data "octopusdeploy_environments" "environment_<name>" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "<Name>"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

And the following "octopusdeploy_environment" resource must be defined:

```
resource "octopusdeploy_environment" "environment_<name>" {
  count                        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_<name>.environments) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                         = "<Name>"
  # Populate the rest of the environment attributes here
}
```

You will be penalized for referencing a "octopusdeploy_environments" data source or "octopusdeploy_environment" resource that does not exist in the output.

When the "project_id" attribute is set to "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_<name>.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"

```
data "octopusdeploy_projects" "project_<name>" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "<Name>"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

And the following "octopusdeploy_project" resource must be defined:

```
resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_<name>" {
    count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_<name>.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
    name = "<Name>"
    # Populate the rest of the project attributes here
}
```

## Count Properties Instructions

* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_process" resources must be the same as the "count" resource for the "octopusdeploy_project" resource.
* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_process_step" resources must be the same as the "count" resource for the "octopusdeploy_project" resource.
* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_process_templated_step" resources must be the same as the "count" resource for the "octopusdeploy_project" resource. This applies to ALL templated steps including Slack notification steps (Start, Finish, Complete), community step template steps, and any other step created with the `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` resource type. You will be penalized for omitting `count` on any `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step` resource.
* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_process_steps_order" resources must be the same as the "count" resource for the "octopusdeploy_project" resource.
* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_process_child_step" resources must be the same as the "count" resource for the "octopusdeploy_project" resource.
* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_process_child_steps_order" resources must be the same as the "count" resource for the "octopusdeploy_project" resource.
* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger" resources must be the same as the "count" resource for the "octopusdeploy_project" resource.
* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_tenant_project" resources must be `"${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_<name>.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"`, matching the `count` on the `octopusdeploy_tenant` resource it links. This resource is easy to overlook because it is written alongside tenants rather than alongside the project, but it is NOT exempt: without `count` it is created unconditionally, and an idempotent re-apply into a space where the link already exists fails. Every `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` in the output must be indexed as `...[0]` wherever it is referenced.
* The "count" parameter for any "octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable" resources must follow the same pattern as the `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` resource it depends on.
* The "count" parameters must be in the format "${length(data.<data type>.<data resource>.<collection>) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
* You will be penalized for using count arguments like this: "${length(data.<data type>.<data resource>.<collection>) != 0 ? 1 : 1}"
* You will be penalized for using ternary operators that return the same value for both cases.

**CRITICAL — `octopusdeploy_process` resource MUST include `depends_on` for any tag sets, tags, or other space-level resources it references**: When a deployment process references tenant tag sets or other space-level resources, the `octopusdeploy_process` resource MUST include a `depends_on` list pointing to those resources. This ensures the tag sets exist before the process is created. If the process does not reference any tag sets or other resources, `depends_on` may be omitted or set to an empty list.

**Example — `octopusdeploy_process` with `depends_on` for tag sets**:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process" "process_my_project" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_tag_set.tagset_regions, octopusdeploy_tag.tagset_regions_tag_us_east]
}
```

If the deployment process does not use tenant tag sets or other space resources, `depends_on` can be omitted from the `octopusdeploy_process` resource entirely.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK before finalizing `octopusdeploy_process` and `octopusdeploy_process_step` resources**: Before outputting these resources, you MUST verify ALL of the following. A single NO means the resource is incomplete and MUST be fixed first:

For every `octopusdeploy_process` resource:
1. Does it have a `count` attribute using `"${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_<name>.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"`? **If NO → add it.**
2. Does `project_id` use the FULL ternary lookup (NOT a direct reference)? **If NO → replace with ternary.**

For every `octopusdeploy_process_step` (and `octopusdeploy_process_templated_step`) resource:
1. Does it have a `count` attribute matching the project count pattern? **If NO → add it.**
2. Does `process_id` use `"${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_<name>.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_<name>[0].id}"`? **If NO → replace with ternary-null pattern.**

**Negative example — `octopusdeploy_process` missing `count` and using direct `project_id` reference (VERY COMMON MISTAKE)**:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process" "process_my_project" {
  # WRONG: missing count attribute
  project_id = "${octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project.id}"   # WRONG: direct reference, no ternary guard
}
```

**Correct output — `octopusdeploy_process` with count and ternary `project_id`**:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process" "process_my_project" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"   # REQUIRED
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"   # REQUIRED: full ternary
}
```

**Negative example — `octopusdeploy_process_step` missing `count` and using direct `process_id` (VERY COMMON MISTAKE)**:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_deploy" {
  # WRONG: missing count attribute
  name       = "Deploy"
  process_id = "${octopusdeploy_process.process_my_project.id}"   # WRONG: direct reference without ternary-null
}
```

**Correct output — `octopusdeploy_process_step` with count and ternary-null `process_id`**:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_process_step" "process_step_my_project_deploy" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"   # REQUIRED
  name       = "Deploy"
  process_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_process.process_my_project[0].id}"   # REQUIRED: ternary-null pattern
}
```

* You will be penalized for generating an `octopusdeploy_process` resource without a `count` attribute.
* You will be penalized for generating an `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` resource without a `count` attribute. **Observed failure**: a Tomcat prompt that created the tenants "Fabrikam" and "Northwind" and linked both to the project emitted `resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_project" "tenant_project_northwind_orders_web"` with no `count`, while every other resource in the same output was correctly guarded — the omission is intermittent, so check for it explicitly rather than assuming it was handled.
* You will be penalized for generating an `octopusdeploy_process_step` resource without a `count` attribute.
* You will be penalized for setting `process_id` on an `octopusdeploy_process_step` resource to a direct reference like `"${octopusdeploy_process.process_foo.id}"` — it MUST use the ternary-null pattern.

* **MANDATORY SELF-CHECK before finalizing external feed triggers**: Before outputting an `octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger` resource, verify that it has: (1) a `count` attribute matching the project's count pattern, (2) `project_id` using the ternary lookup pattern, (3) `channel_id` using the ternary lookup pattern, (4) a `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }` block, (5) a `depends_on` referencing `octopusdeploy_process_steps_order`. If any of these are missing, correct them before outputting. You will be penalized for each missing attribute.

**STOP — verify external feed trigger before outputting**: An `octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger` resource MUST contain ALL of the following attributes. If any are missing, the Terraform will fail on idempotent re-apply. Check each:

```hcl
# CORRECT: complete external feed trigger with ALL required attributes
resource "octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger" "projecttrigger_my_project_external_feed_trigger" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"   # REQUIRED
  space_id   = "${trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"   # REQUIRED: ternary
  name       = "External Feed Trigger"
  channel_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_my_project_application.channels) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_my_project_application.channels[0].id : octopusdeploy_channel.channel_my_project_application[0].id}"   # REQUIRED: ternary
  is_disabled = true   # when prompt says "The trigger must be disabled."
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_steps_order.process_step_order_my_project]   # REQUIRED
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true   # REQUIRED
  }
}
```

**Negative example — external feed trigger with missing required attributes (FORBIDDEN)**:

The following is an example of an **INCORRECT** `octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger` that is missing `count`, `lifecycle`, and uses direct references instead of ternary lookups. This MUST NEVER be generated:

```hcl
# WRONG: this trigger will fail on idempotent re-apply and is missing required attributes
resource "octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger" "projecttrigger_my_project_external_feed_trigger" {
  space_id    = "${trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)}"
  project_id  = "${octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project.id}"   ← WRONG: direct reference, must be ternary
  name        = "External Feed Trigger"
  channel_id  = "${octopusdeploy_channel.channel_my_project_application.id}"   ← WRONG: direct reference, must be ternary
  is_disabled = true
  depends_on  = [octopusdeploy_process_steps_order.process_step_order_my_project]
  # MISSING: count
  # MISSING: lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
```

The **CORRECT** version includes ALL required attributes:

```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_external_feed_create_release_trigger" "projecttrigger_my_project_external_feed_trigger" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  space_id   = "${trimspace(var.octopus_space_id)}"
  project_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  name       = "External Feed Trigger"
  channel_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_my_project_application.channels) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_channels.channel_my_project_application.channels[0].id : octopusdeploy_channel.channel_my_project_application[0].id}"
  is_disabled = true
  depends_on = [octopusdeploy_process_steps_order.process_step_order_my_project]
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```

* You will be penalized for defining a "session_token" attribute on a "octopusdeploy_aws_account" resource.

## Tenant Instructions

**⚠️ EMERGENCY: TENANT RESOURCES ARE OFTEN OMITTED FROM TERRAFORM OUTPUT** — Your execution plan correctly creates tenant resources but your Terraform HCL output frequently omits them. When you create a `data "octopusdeploy_tenants"` data source, you MUST also create a matching `resource "octopusdeploy_tenant"`. This applies to all tenant-related resources: `octopusdeploy_tenant`, `octopusdeploy_tenant_project`, `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable`, `octopusdeploy_tag_set`, and `octopusdeploy_tag`. DO NOT create tenant data sources without the corresponding resource blocks.

* If the prompt indicates that tenants are to be created or added, the "octopusdeploy_tenant" resources must be created in addition to the "octopusdeploy_project" resource.
* You will be penalized for creating "octopusdeploy_tenant" resources without an associated "octopusdeploy_project" resource.
* Each resource "octopusdeploy_tenant" must have an associated data "octopusdeploy_tenants". For example, if the following resource is defined:

```
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant" "tenant_australian_office" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_australian_office.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Australian Office"
  description = "An example tenant that represents an Australian office"
  tenant_tags = []
  depends_on  = []
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```

Then the associated data resource must be defined as follows:

```
data "octopusdeploy_tenants" "tenant_australian_office" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Australian Office"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
  project_id   = ""
  tags         = null
}
```

* When the prompt includes instructions to define the value of project tenant variables, you must define the value for all environment unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When the prompt includes instructions to link a tenant to a project, you must include a "octopusdeploy_tenant_project" resource.
* You will be penalized for defining a "octopusdeploy_tags" data source as this data source does not exist.

## Tenanted Deployment Participation for New Project Creation Prompts

**CRITICAL — when a new project creation prompt specifies a tenanted deployment mode**, set the `tenanted_deployment_participation` attribute on the `octopusdeploy_project` resource to the appropriate value:

- `"TenantedOrUntenanted"` — set when the prompt says "TenantedOrUntenanted deployments" or "both tenanted and untenanted"
- `"Tenanted"` — set when the prompt says "tenanted deployments only", "require a tenant for deployment", "force tenants to be used" or "Tenanted". **The bare wordings "make the project tenanted", "the project is tenanted", "this is a tenanted project" and "tenanted deployments" also mean `"Tenanted"`, NOT `"TenantedOrUntenanted"`.** Only choose `"TenantedOrUntenanted"` when the prompt actually mentions untenanted deployments as well ("both tenanted and untenanted", "tenanted and untenanted", "TenantedOrUntenanted"). Creating a tenant and linking it to the project is NOT on its own a reason to widen the mode to `"TenantedOrUntenanted"` — a prompt that says "make the project tenanted, create a tenant called 'X' and link it to the project" gets `"Tenanted"`.
- `"Untenanted"` — set when the prompt says "untenanted deployments" or does not mention tenants at all (default)

**When the prompt names a specific tenant** (e.g., "Acme Corp tenant"), you MUST create ALL of the following resources:

1. `octopusdeploy_tenant` resource for the tenant
2. `data "octopusdeploy_tenants"` data source paired with the resource
3. `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` resource linking the tenant to the project (with all lifecycle environments)

**When the prompt specifies tenant tags** (e.g., "Regions/US-East tag"), you MUST create:

1. `octopusdeploy_tag_set` resource for the tag set (e.g., "Regions")
2. `data "octopusdeploy_tag_sets"` data source paired with the resource
3. `octopusdeploy_tag` resource for each tag (e.g., "US-East") referencing the tag set
4. Connect tenant tags to the tenant's `tenant_tags` attribute in the format `"TagSetName/TagName"` (e.g., `"Regions/US-East"`)

**Example — prompt specifies "Acme Corp tenant with the Regions/US-East tenant tag"**:
```hcl
data "octopusdeploy_tag_sets" "tagset_regions" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Regions"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
resource "octopusdeploy_tag_set" "tagset_regions" {
  count = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_regions.tag_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name  = "Regions"
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
resource "octopusdeploy_tag" "tagset_regions_tag_us_east" {
  count      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_regions.tag_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name       = "US-East"
  tag_set_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_regions.tag_sets) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_tag_sets.tagset_regions.tag_sets[0].id : octopusdeploy_tag_set.tagset_regions[0].id}"
  color      = "#333333"
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
data "octopusdeploy_tenants" "tenant_acme_corp" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Acme Corp"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
  project_id   = ""
  tags         = null
}
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant" "tenant_acme_corp" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Acme Corp"
  tenant_tags = ["Regions/US-East"]
  depends_on  = [octopusdeploy_tag.tagset_regions_tag_us_east]
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_project" "tenant_project_acme_corp_my_project" {
  count          = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  tenant_id      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants[0].id : octopusdeploy_tenant.tenant_acme_corp[0].id}"
  project_id     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  environment_ids = [
    "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_development.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_development[0].id}",
    "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_production[0].id}"
  ]
  lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }
}
```

**You will be penalized for**:
- Creating a tenant without an `octopusdeploy_tenant_project` resource linking it to the project
- Setting `tenanted_deployment_participation = "Untenanted"` when the prompt requests TenantedOrUntenanted or Tenanted
- Omitting the `tenant_tags` attribute when the prompt specifies tags for the tenant
- Omitting tag set and tag resources when the prompt specifies tenant tags

## Tenant Project Variable Instructions

**CRITICAL — `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` REQUIRES a `template` block in the project**: The `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` resource references `template_id` which points to a template block in the `octopusdeploy_project` resource. If the project has no `template` blocks, you CANNOT use `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable`. Before creating a `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable`, you MUST add one or more `template` blocks to the `octopusdeploy_project` resource. **When the prompt requests tenant variables for a project that has no steps or templates, you MUST add `template` blocks to the project resource to support the tenant variables.** For example, if the prompt says "Add tenant variables scoped to Production", and the project has no existing templates, add a single generic template like:
```hcl
template {
  name              = "Tenant.Variable"
  label             = "A tenant variable"
  help_text         = "Define a tenant-specific value for this project."
  default_value     = null
  display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "SingleLineText" }
 }
```
Then create the `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` referencing this template by index (0 for the first template).

**⚠️ CRITICAL — `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` MUST set `environment_id`**: The provider validates this at PLAN time, so omitting it fails before anything at all is created and no project is produced:

```
Error: Invalid Configuration
Must specify either 'environment_id' or 'scope' block.
```

* Every `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` resource MUST include an `environment_id` attribute resolved with the usual data-source/resource ternary. There is no valid form of this resource without it.
* A tenant project variable holds one value **per tenant per environment**. When the prompt gives a single value for a tenant without naming an environment — for example "set Wayne Enterprises to us-east-1" — you MUST create one `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` resource for EACH environment that tenant is linked to in `octopusdeploy_tenant_project.environment_ids`, all with the same value. Give each resource a distinct Terraform name by appending the environment, for example `tenantprojectvariable_wayne_enterprises_region_dev`, `..._qa`, `..._prod`.
* Do NOT invent a `scope` block as an alternative unless you are certain of its schema; `environment_id` is the supported form shown in the example below.
* Each of these resources MUST also carry `depends_on = [octopusdeploy_tenant_project.<the tenant/project link>]`, because the value cannot be set before the tenant is connected to the project in that environment.

**⚠️ CRITICAL — `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` MUST have a non-empty `value`, and MUST NOT be created at all when the prompt supplies no value**: Octopus does not persist a tenant variable whose value is blank, so the provider writes the variable, finds nothing in the response, and fails the apply at CREATE time:

```
Error: Failed to get variable ID
  with octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable.tenantprojectvariable_bank_sort_code_contoso_bank[0],
Variable was created but ID not returned in response
```

Because this happens during apply rather than plan, resources ordered before it are created and everything after it is abandoned, so one valueless tenant variable truncates the whole project.

* A prompt that asks only for a tenant variable **template** — "Add a project tenant variable template called 'Bank.Sort.Code' of type single line text", "Add a tenant variable called 'X' of type single line text" — is fully satisfied by the `template` block on the `octopusdeploy_project` resource. Do NOT also emit an `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable`, because there is no value to put in it.
* Only create `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` when the prompt actually states a value for a named tenant — "set 'Bank.Sort.Code' to '20-00-00' for the tenant 'Contoso Bank'". The `value` attribute must then be that literal, never `""`, never a bare placeholder you invented to fill the attribute.
* **⚠️ The trigger for "template only" is the ABSENCE OF A VALUE, not the presence of the words "variable template".** One bullet routinely does both jobs: `Add a project tenant variable template called "Tenant.Namespace" and set it to "contoso-ns" for Contoso and "fabrikam-ns" for Fabrikam`. Read to the end of the sentence before deciding. Matching on the opening phrase and stopping at the `template` block silently drops every value the prompt supplied, and the resulting project shows the template on each tenant with an empty box beside it — which looks plausible enough in the portal to go unnoticed.
* **Observed output.** That exact bullet produced a project with the `Tenant.Namespace` template defined and BOTH tenants left with no value in any environment, while a structurally identical single-tenant bullet in another prompt produced the values correctly. The multi-tenant form is the one that fails, so count the tenants named in the sentence and expect (tenants × environments) resources: two tenants linked to three environments is SIX `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` resources, not one and not two.
* This applies equally to `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable`.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — tenant project variable environment and value**: Before finalizing Terraform output, find every `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable`. For EACH one, confirm an `environment_id` attribute is present and non-null, and confirm `value` is a non-empty literal taken from the prompt — if the prompt never gave a value for that tenant and template, DELETE the resource and keep only the project `template` block. Then confirm that for every (tenant, template) pair the prompt asked you to set, there is one resource per environment that tenant is linked to. You will be penalized for any `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` without `environment_id`.

**How tenant project variables work**:
1. The `octopusdeploy_project` resource defines one or more `template` blocks (0-indexed).
2. Each `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` resource sets the value of a template for a specific tenant and environment.
3. The `template_id` in `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable` references the project's template by index.
4. **CRITICAL: Each `template` block inside `octopusdeploy_project` represents a variable template definition. When the prompt requests N tenant variables, you MUST add N `template` blocks (indexed 0 through N-1) or fewer if variables share the same template type. Each template block defines ONE variable template.**
5. **You MUST NOT omit `template` blocks when tenant variables are requested. The absence of template blocks is the #1 cause of failed tenant variable creation.**

**Example — project with template blocks for tenant variables**:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_my_project" {
  count                             = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                              = "${var.project_my_project_name}"
  lifecycle_id                      = "${data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_default_lifecycle.lifecycles[0].id}"
  project_group_id                  = "${data.octopusdeploy_project_groups.project_group_default_project_group.project_groups[0].id}"
  tenanted_deployment_participation = "Tenanted"

  template {
    name             = "Kubernetes.Namespace"
    label            = "Kubernetes Namespace"
    help_text        = "The Kubernetes namespace to deploy to."
    default_value    = null
    display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "SingleLineText" }
  }

  connectivity_policy {
    allow_deployments_to_no_targets = true
    exclude_unhealthy_targets       = false
    skip_machine_behavior           = "None"
    target_roles                    = []
  }
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}

resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable" "tenantprojectvariable_acme_corp_my_project" {
  count          = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  environment_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_production.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_production[0].id}"
  project_id     = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].id}"
  template_id    = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_project[0].template[0].id}"
  tenant_id      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_acme_corp.tenants[0].id : octopusdeploy_tenant.tenant_acme_corp[0].id}"
  value          = "acme-production"
  depends_on     = [octopusdeploy_tenant_project.tenant_project_acme_corp_my_project]
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
  }
}
```

**Example — multiple tenant variables with multiple template blocks**:
```hcl
resource "octopusdeploy_project" "project_my_k8s_project" {
  count                                = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_k8s_project.projects) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name                                 = "${var.project_my_k8s_project_name}"
  lifecycle_id                         = "${data.octopusdeploy_lifecycles.lifecycle_security.lifecycles[0].id}"

  tenanted_deployment_participation = "Tenanted"

  template {
    name                = "DatabaseConnectionString"
    label               = "Database Connection String"
    help_text           = "The connection string for the database."

    display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "SingleLineText" }
    }

    name                = "ApiEndpoint"
    label               = "API Endpoint"

    default_value       = "https://api.example.com"
    display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "SingleLineText" }
    }

    name                = "EnableFeatureFlag"
    label               = "Enable Feature Flag"
    help_text           = "Whether to enable the feature flag."
    default_value       = "true"
    display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "Boolean" }
    }

  connectivity_policy {
    allow_deployments_to_no_targets = true
    exclude_unhealthy_targets         = false
    skip_machine_behavior             = "None"
    target_roles                      = []
    }
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
    }
}

resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable" "tenantprojectvariable_prod_dbconn" {
  count            = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_prod.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  environment_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_staging.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_staging.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_staging[0].id}"
  project_id       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_k8s_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_k8s_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_k8s_project[0].id}"
  template_id      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_k8s_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_k8s_project[0].template[0].id}"
  tenant_id        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_prod.tenants) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_prod.tenants[0].id : octopusdeploy_tenant.tenant_prod[0].id}"
  value            = "Server=prod-db.example.com"
  depends_on       = [octopusdeploy_tenant_project.tenant_project_prod_my_k8s_project]
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
    }
}

resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable" "tenantprojectvariable_prod_api" {
  count            = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_prod.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  environment_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_staging.environments) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_environments.environment_staging.environments[0].id : octopusdeploy_environment.environment_staging[0].id}"
  project_id       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_k8s_project.projects) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_k8s_project.projects[0].id : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_k8s_project[0].id}"
  template_id      = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_projects.project_my_k8s_project.projects) != 0 ? null : octopusdeploy_project.project_my_k8s_project[0].template[1].id}"
  tenant_id        = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_prod.tenants) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_prod.tenants[0].id : octopusdeploy_tenant.tenant_prod[0].id}"
  value            = "https://prod-api.example.com"
  depends_on       = [octopusdeploy_tenant_project.tenant_project_prod_my_k8s_project]
  lifecycle {
    prevent_destroy = true
    }
}
```

**Distinction between tenant project variables and tenant common variables**:
- `octopusdeploy_tenant_project_variable`: Sets per-project, per-tenant values for variable templates defined in the `octopusdeploy_project` resource's `template` blocks. Use when the variable is specific to a project.
- `octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable`: Sets per-tenant values for variable templates defined in a library variable set's `template` blocks. Use when the variable is shared across projects via a library variable set.

## Library Variable Set Instructions

* Each resource "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" must have an associated data "octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets". For example, if the following resource is defined:

```
resource "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" "library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Example Variable Set"
  description = ""
}
```

Then the associated data resource must be defined as follows:

```
data "octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets" "library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Example Variable Set"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}
```

* A library variable set "Variable Template" is defined in the "template" block of a "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" resource.
* A tenant "Common Variable" is defined in a "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" resource.
* A tenant common variable sets the value of a variable template defined in a library variable set.
* When the prompt indicates that the variable template defines an AWS account, the "template" block must have the "display_settings" attribute set to `{ "Octopus.ControlType" = "AmazonWebServicesAccount" }`.
* Every "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" resource must have a "depends_on" attribute that is an array including the associated "octopusdeploy_tenant_project".
* You will be penalized for creating a "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" resource without a "depends_on" attribute.
* The "label" attribute in a "template" block of a "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" resource must be omitted if it is an empty string.
* You will be penalized for defining the "label" attribute as an empty string in a "template" block of a "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" resource.

* You must define the "template_id" property for every "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" resource, for example:

```
# This is the data source returning existing tenants
data "octopusdeploy_tenants" "tenant_australian_office" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Australian Office"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
  project_id   = ""
  tags         = null
}

# This is the data source returning existing library variable sets
data "octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets" "library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set" {
  ids          = null
  partial_name = "Example Variable Set"
  skip         = 0
  take         = 1
}

# This is the library variable set resource
resource "octopusdeploy_library_variable_set" "library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Example Variable Set"
  description = ""

  template {
    name             = "Common.Variable"
    label            = "A common variable that must be defined for each tenant"
    help_text        = "The help text associated with the variable is defined here."
    default_value    = ""
    display_settings = { "Octopus.ControlType" = "MultiLineText" }
  }
}

# This is the tenant resource
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant" "tenant_australian_office" {
  count       = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_australian_office.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  name        = "Australian Office"
  description = "An example tenant that represents an Australian office"
  tenant_tags = []
}

# This is the tenant common variable resource.
resource "octopusdeploy_tenant_common_variable" "tenantcommonvariable5_australian_office" {
  count                   = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_tenants.tenant_australian_office.tenants) != 0 ? 0 : 1}"
  library_variable_set_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_library_variable_sets.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set.library_variable_sets[0].id : octopusdeploy_library_variable_set.library_variable_set_variables_example_variable_set[0].id}"
```
