* You must use the "Run Octopus Deploy Runbook" step template to run a runbook from a deployment process unless otherwise specified in the prompt.

## Blocking release progression

* Use the "Block Release Progression" community step template from "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/78a182b3-5369-4e13-9292-b7f991295ad1" to block a release from progressing through environments.
* You must use the "Block Release Progression" step template to block a release from progressing through environments unless otherwise specified in the prompt.

## Kubernetes target

**When this pattern applies — read this carefully, it is under-triggered.** Apply the canonical pattern below whenever the project contains ANY Kubernetes step (`Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`, `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade`, or any step with `Octopus.Action.TargetRoles` intended for a cluster) AND the prompt does not supply a real cluster endpoint together with real credentials. In particular, ALL of the following still require the canonical pattern — none of them counts as "a specific target configuration":

* The prompt only names a target tag or role, for example "deploy to targets tagged `Kubernetes`" or "using targets tagged `octopub/frontend`". A tag names *which* target to use; it does not configure one.
* The prompt says "deploy to a Kubernetes cluster", "deploy to EKS/AKS/GKE", or names a cluster without giving its URL.
* The prompt asks for an account — a Token, AWS, Azure, or username/password account — but gives no cluster URL. Creating the account does NOT remove the need for a target. When the prompt names its own account (for example a Token account called "K8s"), use THAT account on the "Mock K8s" target instead of creating "Mock Token", and skip creating "Mock Token" entirely.
* The prompt is a canary, blue/green, progressive, wave, or rollout prompt that implies a cluster.

A "specific target configuration" means the prompt gives an actual cluster URL (or explicitly says to use an existing named target). Only then may you skip the pattern.

You will be penalized for generating a project containing Kubernetes steps with no Kubernetes target, and for leaving `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck`, `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait`, and `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled` at their template defaults on an `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml` step when the "Mock K8s" target is in use. The three defaults that MUST be overridden are `ResourceStatusCheck = "False"`, `DeploymentWait = "NoWait"`, and `ServerSideApply.Enabled = "False"`; a raw-YAML step left with `ResourceStatusCheck = "True"` or `ServerSideApply.Enabled = "True"` will fail against "Mock K8s". You will equally be penalized for writing any of these three properties onto an `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` step, where none of them belong.

**Which of the three properties each Kubernetes step type accepts** (taken from the `everystep.tf` example configuration — do not add a property to a step type that is not ticked here):

| Property | `KubernetesDeployRawYaml` | `Kubernetes.Kustomize` | `HelmChartUpgrade` | `KubernetesRunScript` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck` | yes | yes | yes | no |
| `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled` (and `.ForceConflicts`) | yes | yes | no | no |
| `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait` | yes | no | no | no |

* So when a prompt says "use client side apply" or "disable verification checks" for a project whose steps include Kustomize or Helm, set `ResourceStatusCheck = "False"` on the raw-YAML, Kustomize AND Helm steps, and `ServerSideApply.Enabled = "False"` on the raw-YAML and Kustomize steps only. Leave all three off `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript`.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — Kubernetes target and step properties**: Before finalizing Terraform output, ask: does this project contain any Kubernetes step? If YES, verify that (1) an `octopusdeploy_kubernetes_cluster_deployment_target` resource exists — this is required by EVERY Kubernetes step type, `Octopus.KubernetesRunScript` and `Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade` included — and (2) every step of type `Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml`, and no other step type, sets `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck = "False"`, `Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait = "NoWait"`, and `Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled = "False"` in `execution_properties`. Add whatever is missing from the raw-YAML steps, and remove any of the three that has leaked into a kubectl script or Helm step.

* When the prompt indicates that a Kubernetes target is required but does not provide a specific target configuration, use the following canonical pattern:
    * YOU MUST create a token account called "Mock Token".
    * The target MUST use the "Mock Token" account for authentication, for example:
    ```
     authentication {
        account_id = "${length(data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_token.accounts) != 0 ? data.octopusdeploy_accounts.account_token.accounts[0].id : octopusdeploy_token_account.account_token[0].id}"
      }
    ```
    * You will be penalized for using any other account type (e.g., AWS, Azure, username/password) for the "Mock K8s" target.
    * YOU MUST link the token account to any tenants.
    * **⚠️ NAME THE TARGET AFTER THE TARGET TAG IT SERVES when the project uses any target tag other than plain "Kubernetes".** Call it `Mock K8s <target tag>` — for example a project whose steps target `llm-cluster` gets a target named "Mock K8s llm-cluster". Naming every mock target "Mock K8s" breaks the moment a space already contains one: the `data "octopusdeploy_deployment_targets"` lookup finds the existing "Mock K8s", `count` evaluates to 0, no target is created, and the target that DOES exist carries some other project's tags. The apply succeeds and the new project is left with no target that matches its steps at all — the exact failure the whole pattern exists to prevent. This has been observed: a project deploying to `llm-cluster` reused a "Mock K8s" target whose roles were `["Kubernetes", "storefront-cluster"]`.
      * The `roles` list still contains EVERY target tag the project's Kubernetes steps use, plus `"Kubernetes"`.
      * The data source paired with the target uses the same tag-qualified `partial_name`, so re-running the same prompt is still idempotent.
      * Use the bare name "Mock K8s" only when the project's Kubernetes steps use no target tag other than "Kubernetes".
    * YOU MUST create a feed called "Docker Hub" pointing to "https://index.docker.io" using anonymous authentication.
    * You will be penalized for defining any authentication credentials for the "Docker Hub" feed. It must be anonymous.
    * YOU MUST add a target called "Mock K8s", with the tag "Kubernetes", using the token account, pointing to "https://mockk8s.octopusdemos.com", using the health check image "octopusdeploy/worker-tools:6.5.0-ubuntu.22.04" from the "Docker Hub" feed, using the worker pool "Hosted Ubuntu".
    * YOU MUST link the target to any tenants.
    * YOU MUST disable step verification in all "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" steps by setting the "Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ResourceStatusCheck" property in the "execution_properties" block to "False" - the "Mock K8s" target DOES NOT support verification.
    * YOU MUST set the "Octopus.Action.KubernetesContainers.DeploymentWait" property in the "execution_properties" block of all "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" steps to "NoWait" - the "Mock K8s" target DOES NOT support deployment wait.
    * YOU MUST use client side apply for all Kubernetes YAML steps by setting the "Octopus.Action.Kubernetes.ServerSideApply.Enabled" property in the "execution_properties" block to "False" - the "Mock K8s" target DOES NOT support server side apply.
    * These three properties are for "Octopus.KubernetesDeployRawYaml" steps ONLY. Do NOT add them to "Octopus.KubernetesRunScript" (kubectl script) steps or to "Octopus.HelmChartUpgrade" steps — those step types do not use them. They still need the "Mock K8s" target itself.

## Blue/Green deployment pattern

* When the prompt indicates that Blue/Green deployment pattern is required:
    *You MUST add the step template from "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/72db001f-ae7f-4a0f-b952-5f80e2fc4cd2" as the first step. It is called "Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment" by default. This step determines if the deployment to two environments, the blue and the green environment, have been correctly performed in an alternating pattern or not.
    * The output variable of the "Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment" step is a boolean value called `SequentialDeploy`. If `SequentialDeploy` is `True`, the deployment was performed incorrectly to the same blue or green environment twice in a row. If the value is `False`, the deployment was performed correctly to the blue and green environments in an alternating pattern.
    * You MUST add a manual intervention step as the second step with a run condition based on the `SequentialDeploy` output variable of the first step.
    * **⚠️ CRITICAL — the run condition is a STEP-LEVEL `Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression` property, NOT text inside the manual intervention instructions.** The manual step resource MUST set `condition = "Variable"` together with `"Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression" = "#{Octopus.Action[Octopus - Check Blue Green Deployment].Output.SequentialDeploy}"`. Embedding an `#{if Octopus.Action[...].Output.SequentialDeploy}…#{/if}` block in `Octopus.Action.Manual.Instructions` does NOT gate the step — the step then runs on every deployment and only its wording changes, which defeats the entire purpose of the check. Setting `condition = "Success"` on this step is always wrong.
    * When the project type template already contains its own manual intervention step (for example `Approve Production Deployment` in the VM Blue/Green template), that step is NOT a substitute for the pattern's gate. Either add the `SequentialDeploy` variable condition to it, or add a separate manual step that carries the condition. A Blue/Green project whose process contains no step with `condition = "Variable"` is incomplete.
    * You MUST replace the "Production" environment from the template project with the "Blue Production" and "Green Production" environments.
    * **⚠️ CRITICAL — "replace" means the plain `Production` environment MUST NOT remain.** Do not create a `Production` environment resource, and do not leave `Production` in any lifecycle phase alongside the blue and green environments. A phase holding `["Production", "Blue Production", "Green Production"]` is wrong: releases can then be deployed straight to `Production`, bypassing the alternation the pattern exists to enforce. When the prompt says "containing the production environments", that means the blue and green pair.
    * **⚠️ CRITICAL — each production stack lives in its OWN `phase` block, and BOTH stack phases must be optional.** Give `Blue Production` its own `phase` and `Green Production` its own `phase`, with exactly one environment in each `optional_deployment_targets`, and set `is_optional_phase = true` on BOTH. One environment per phase — do NOT merge the two stacks into a single phase and do NOT put both in one `optional_deployment_targets` list. Separate optional stack phases are what let a release be promoted to one stack without the other (see the "parallel production stacks" rule and the "Example Octopus VM Blue/Green Terraform Configuration"). Keep a pre-production phase (e.g. `Development`/`Test`) `is_optional_phase = false` so the lifecycle stays valid.

**MANDATORY SELF-CHECK — Blue/Green completeness**: before finalizing a Blue/Green project, verify ALL of the following, because each has been observed missing in otherwise-successful output:
  1. Exactly two production environments exist, named `Blue Production` and `Green Production`, and no plain `Production` environment is created or referenced by the lifecycle.
  2. `Blue Production` is alone in one `phase` and `Green Production` is alone in another `phase`, and BOTH phases are `is_optional_phase = true` (the two stacks must NOT be merged into a single phase).
  3. `BlueGreen.Environment.Blue.Name` and `BlueGreen.Environment.Green.Name` hold those two DIFFERENT names.
  4. A project variable backs whatever `BlueGreen.Octopus.Api.Key` references.
  5. **At least one step has `condition = "Variable"` with `Octopus.Step.ConditionVariableExpression` referencing `…Output.SequentialDeploy`.** If no step in your output has `condition = "Variable"`, the pattern's gate is missing — add the manual intervention step now. This is required even when the prompt supplies its own numbered list of steps and says "and no others"; the gate is one of the pattern steps the prompt's list does not override.
    * The step template's `BlueGreen.Environment.Blue.Name` and `BlueGreen.Environment.Green.Name` parameters MUST be set to the two blue/green environment names ("Blue Production" and "Green Production") respectively. They MUST NOT be equal to each other, and neither may be left as "Production".
    * The step template's `BlueGreen.Octopus.Api.Key` parameter references a project variable, conventionally `#{Project.Octopus.Api.Key}`. Whatever variable name you reference here, you MUST also create a matching sensitive `octopusdeploy_variable` on the project (with a placeholder value), otherwise the step is left with an unresolvable reference.
* These steps are IN ADDITION to the steps from the template project.

## Debugging

* When the prompt indicates that debugging is required but does not provide specific instructions, use the following canonical pattern:
    * Add two project variable called "OctopusPrintVariables", set the description to "Prints variable values to the verbose logs", and set the value to "True".
    * Add the project variable called "OctopusPrintEvaluatedVariables", set the description to "Prints evaluated variable values to the verbose logs", and set the value to "True".
    * Set the `default_guided_failure_mode` to `On` in the `octopusdeploy_project` resource. This ensures that the deployment will fail if any step fails, allowing for easier debugging.

## Rollback or redeployments

* When the prompt indicates that only some steps are required to be run as part of a rollback or redeployments are required but does not provide specific instructions, sdd the community step template with the url "https://library.octopus.com/step-templates/d166457a-1421-4731-b143-dd6766fb95d5" as the first step. This step is called "Calculate Deployment Mode" by default.
* The "Calculate Deployment Mode" step will determine if the deployment is a rollback, redeployment, or a new deployment. It will also determine if the deployment was caused by a trigger or is a manual deployment. It will also determine the version difference between the previous release and the current release.
* Use the output variables documented below to determine if steps that must only be run on a rollback or redeployment should be run or not.

### Deployment Mode
The potential modes are:
- **Deploy**: A newer version is being deployed to the target environment.  For example, `2021.1.4` is being deployed to **Production** to replace `2021.0.5`.
- **Rollback**: An older version is being deployed to the target environment. For example, `2021.0.5` is being deployed to **Production** to replace `2021.1.4`.
- **Redeploy**: The same version is being deployed to the target environment.  For example, `2021.1.4` is being deployed to **Production** which already has `2021.1.4`.

**Please note**: This step template uses the release numbers to calculate the deployment mode.  It doesn't look at any packages.

### Version Difference
After calculating the deployment mode, the step template will calculate the version difference.  The potential options are:
- **Identical**: No differences between the previous release and the current release were found.
- **Major**: The first number (2021 in 2021.1.2.10) is different between the previous release and the current release.
- **Minor**: The second number (1 in 2021.1.2.10) is different between the previous release and the current release.
- **Build**: The third number (2 in 2021.1.2.10) is different between the previous release and the current release.
- **Revision**: The fourth number (10 in 2021.1.2.10) is different between the previous release and the current release.

### Manual or Automatic Trigger
The step template will also determine if the deployment was caused by a trigger or is a manual deployment.  Potential values are `True` (caused by a trigger) or `False` (manual deployment).

### Output Variables

The following output variables will be set:
- **DeploymentMode**: Will either be `Deploy`, `Rollback` or `Redeploy`.
- **Trigger**: Will either be `True` or `False`.  Indicates if this deployment was caused by a trigger (scheduled or deployment target).
- **VersionChange**: Will either be `Identical`, `Major`, `Minor`, `Build`, or `Revision`.

##### Variable Run Condition Output Variables
To make it easier to use, the step template will set a number of run condition output variables.

##### Variable Run Condition Usage
Variable Run Conditions will _always_ be evaluated.  Even if there is an error.  If the run condition comes back as **Truthy** it will run the step.

To limit when the step runs, wrap the output variable with an if/then or unless clause:
- **Always Run**: `#{Octopus.Action[Calculate Deployment Mode].Output.RunOnDeploy}`
- **Success**: Only run when previous steps succeeds `#{unless Octopus.Deployment.Error}#{Octopus.Action[Calculate Deployment Mode].Output.RunOnDeploy}#{/unless}`
- **Failure**: Only run when previous steps fail `#{if Octopus.Deployment.Error}#{Octopus.Action[Calculate Deployment Mode].Output.RunOnDeploy}#{/if}`

**Hint:** Replace **RunOnDeploy** from the above examples with one of the variables from below.

##### Deployment Mode Run Conditions
- **RunOnDeploy**: Only run the step when the **DeploymentMode** is `Deploy`.
- **RunOnRollback**: Only run the step when the **DeploymentMode** is `Rollback`.
- **RunOnRedeploy**: Only run the step when the **DeploymentMode** is `Redeploy`.
- **RunOnDeployOrRollback**: Only run the step when the **DeploymentMode** is`Deploy` or `Rollback`.
- **RunOnDeployOrRedeploy**: Only run the step when the **DeploymentMode** is`Deploy` or `Redeploy`.
- **RunOnRedeployOrRollback**: Only run the step when the **DeploymentMode** is `Redeploy` or `Rollback`.

##### Version Change Run Conditions
- **RunOnMajorVersionChange**: Only run the step when the **VersionChange** is `Major`.
- **RunOnMinorVersionChange**: Only run the step when the **VersionChange** is `Minor`.
- **RunOnMajorOrMinorVersionChange**: Only run the step when the **VersionChange** is `Major` or `Minor`.
- **RunOnBuildVersionChange**: Only run the step when the **VersionChange** is `Build`.
- **RunOnRevisionVersionChange**: Only run the step when the **VersionChange** is `Revision`.

### Usage

**Important:** This step template is designed for deployment processes only.  Runbooks have no concept of deployments, redeployments, or rollbacks.

This step was designed to run on a worker (or the Octopus Server).  It can run on targets, but the output variables will all be the same; running on targets will do nothing but waste compute cycles.
